<a href="#term_weblog">weblog</a> |  <a href="#term_workInfoHomepage">workInfoHomepage</a> |  <a href="#term_workplaceHomepage">workplaceHomepage</a> |  <a href="#term_yahooChatID">yahooChatID</a> | \n</p>\n</div>\n\n<!-- and this is the bulk of the vocab descriptions -->\n<div class="termlist"><h3>Classes and Properties (full detail)</h3>\n<div class=\'termdetails\'><br />\n\n<h2>Classes</h2>\n <div class="specterm classterm " id="term_Agent" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Agent" typeof="rdfs:Class">\n            <h3>Class: foaf:Agent</h3> \n            <em>Agent</em> - An agent (eg. person, group, software or physical artifact). <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >stable</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Properties include:</th>\n <td>  <a href="#term_gender">gender</a>\n <a href="#term_yahooChatID">yahooChatID</a>\n <a href="#term_account">account</a>\n <a href="#term_birthday">birthday</a>\n <a href="#term_icqChatID">icqChatID</a>\n <a href="#term_aimChatID">aimChatID</a>\n <a href="#term_jabberID">jabberID</a>\n <a href="#term_made">made</a>\n <a href="#term_mbox">mbox</a>\n <a href="#term_interest">interest</a>\n <a href="#term_tipjar">tipjar</a>\n <a href="#term_skypeID">skypeID</a>\n <a href="#term_topic_interest">topic_interest</a>\n <a href="#term_age">age</a>\n <a href="#term_mbox_sha1sum">mbox_sha1sum</a>\n <a href="#term_status">status</a>\n <a href="#term_msnChatID">msnChatID</a>\n <a href="#term_openid">openid</a>\n <a href="#term_holdsAccount">holdsAccount</a>\n <a href="#term_weblog">weblog</a>\n </td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Used with:</th>\n <td>  <a href="#term_maker">maker</a>\n <a href="#term_member">member</a>\n</td></tr> <tr><th>Has Subclass</th>\n <td>  <a href="#term_Group">Group</a>\n <a href="#term_Person">Person</a>\n <a href="#term_Organization">Organization</a>\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_Agent\'>Agent</a></code> class is the class of agents; things that do stuff. A well \nknown sub-class is <code><a href=\'#term_Person\'>Person</a></code>, representing people. Other kinds of agents \ninclude <code><a href=\'#term_Organization\'>Organization</a></code> and <code><a href=\'#term_Group\'>Group</a></code>.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_Agent\'>Agent</a></code> class is useful in a few places in FOAF where \n<code><a href=\'#term_Person\'>Person</a></code> would have been overly specific. For example, the IM chat ID \nproperties such as <code>jabberID</code> are typically associated with people, but \nsometimes belong to software bots.\n</p>\n\n<!-- todo: write rdfs:domain statements for those properties -->\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_Agent">#</a>] <!-- Agent Agent --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm classterm " id="term_Document" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Document" typeof="rdfs:Class">\n            <h3>Class: foaf:Document</h3> \n            <em>Document</em> - A document. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >stable</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Properties include:</th>\n <td>  <a href="#term_topic">topic</a>\n <a href="#term_primaryTopic">primaryTopic</a>\n <a href="#term_sha1">sha1</a>\n </td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Used with:</th>\n <td>  <a href="#term_workInfoHomepage">workInfoHomepage</a>\n <a href="#term_workplaceHomepage">workplaceHomepage</a>\n <a href="#term_page">page</a>\n <a href="#term_accountServiceHomepage">accountServiceHomepage</a>\n <a href="#term_openid">openid</a>\n <a href="#term_tipjar">tipjar</a>\n <a href="#term_schoolHomepage">schoolHomepage</a>\n <a href="#term_publications">publications</a>\n <a href="#term_isPrimaryTopicOf">isPrimaryTopicOf</a>\n <a href="#term_interest">interest</a>\n <a href="#term_homepage">homepage</a>\n <a href="#term_weblog">weblog</a>\n</td></tr> <tr><th>Has Subclass</th>\n <td>  <a href="#term_Image">Image</a>\n <a href="#term_PersonalProfileDocument">PersonalProfileDocument</a>\n </td></tr>\n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr><tr><th>Disjoint With:</th>\n <td>  <span rel="owl:disjointWith" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Project"><a href="#term_Project">Project</a></span>\n <span rel="owl:disjointWith" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Organization"><a href="#term_Organization">Organization</a></span>\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_Document\'>Document</a></code> class represents those things which are, broadly conceived, \n\'documents\'.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_Image\'>Image</a></code> class is a sub-class of <code><a href=\'#term_Document\'>Document</a></code>, since all images \nare documents.\n</p>\n\n<p class="editorial">\nWe do not (currently) distinguish precisely between physical and electronic documents, or \nbetween copies of a work and the abstraction those copies embody. The relationship between \ndocuments and their byte-stream representation needs clarification (see <code><a href=\'#term_sha1\'>sha1</a></code> \nfor related issues).\n</p>\n\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_Document">#</a>] <!-- Document Document --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm classterm " id="term_Group" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Group" typeof="rdfs:Class">\n            <h3>Class: foaf:Group</h3> \n            <em>Group</em> - A class of Agents. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >stable</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Properties include:</th>\n <td>  <a href="#term_member">member</a>\n </td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Subclass Of</th>\n <td>  <span rel="rdfs:subClassOf" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Agent"><a href="#term_Agent">Agent</a></span>\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_Group\'>Group</a></code> class represents a collection of individual agents (and may \nitself play the role of a <code><a href=\'#term_Agent\'>Agent</a></code>, ie. something that can perform actions).\n</p>\n<p>\nThis concept is intentionally quite broad, covering informal and \nad-hoc groups, long-lived communities, organizational groups within a workplace, etc. Some \nsuch groups may have associated characteristics which could be captured in RDF (perhaps a \n<code><a href=\'#term_homepage\'>homepage</a></code>, <code><a href=\'#term_name\'>name</a></code>, mailing list etc.).\n</p>\n\n<p>\nWhile a <code><a href=\'#term_Group\'>Group</a></code> has the characteristics of a <code><a href=\'#term_Agent\'>Agent</a></code>, it \nis also associated with a number of other <code><a href=\'#term_Agent\'>Agent</a></code>s (typically people) who \nconstitute the <code><a href=\'#term_Group\'>Group</a></code>. FOAF provides a mechanism, the \n<code><a href=\'#term_membershipClass\'>membershipClass</a></code> property, which relates a <code><a href=\'#term_Group\'>Group</a></code> to a \nsub-class of the class <code><a href=\'#term_Agent\'>Agent</a></code> who are members of the group. This is a \nlittle complicated, but allows us to make group membership rules explicit.\n</p>\n\n\n<p>The markup (shown below) for defining a group is both complex and powerful. It \nallows group membership rules to match against any RDF-describable characteristics of the potential \ngroup members. As FOAF and similar vocabularies become more expressive in their ability to \ndescribe individuals, the <code><a href=\'#term_Group\'>Group</a></code> mechanism for categorising them into \ngroups also becomes more powerful. \n</p>\n\n<p> \nWhile the formal description of membership criteria for a <code><a href=\'#term_Group\'>Group</a></code> may\nbe complex, the basic mechanism for saying that someone is in a <code><a href=\'#term_Group\'>Group</a></code> is\nvery simple. We simply use a <code><a href=\'#term_member\'>member</a></code> property of the\n<code><a href=\'#term_Group\'>Group</a></code> to indicate the agents that are members of the group. For example:\n\n</p> \n\n<div class="example"> \n\n<pre> \n&lt;foaf:Group&gt;\n &lt;foaf:name&gt;ILRT staff&lt;/foaf:name&gt;\n &lt;foaf:member&gt;\n  &lt;foaf:Person&gt;\n   &lt;foaf:name&gt;Martin Poulter&lt;/foaf:name&gt;\n   &lt;foaf:homepage rdf:resource="http://www.ilrt.bris.ac.uk/aboutus/staff/staffprofile/?search=plmlp"/&gt;\n   &lt;foaf:workplaceHomepage rdf:resource="http://www.ilrt.bris.ac.uk/"/&gt;\n  &lt;/foaf:Person&gt;\n &lt;/foaf:member&gt;\n&lt;/foaf:Group&gt;\n</pre>\n</div>\n\n\n\n<p>\nBehind the scenes, further RDF statements can be used to express the rules for being a \nmember of this group. End-users of FOAF need not pay attention to these details.\n</p>\n\n\n<p>\nHere is an example. We define a <code><a href=\'#term_Group\'>Group</a></code> representing those people who \nare ILRT staff members (ILRT is a department at the University of Bristol). The <code><a href=\'#term_membershipClass\'>membershipClass</a></code> property connects the group (conceived of as a social \nentity and agent in its own right) with the class definition for those people who \nconstitute it. In this case, the rule is that all group members are in the \nILRTStaffPerson class, which is in turn populated by all those things that are a \n<code><a href=\'#term_Person\'>Person</a></code> and which have a <code><a href=\'#term_workplaceHomepage\'>workplaceHomepage</a></code> of \nhttp://www.ilrt.bris.ac.uk/. This is typical: FOAF groups are created by \nspecifying a sub-class of <code><a href=\'#term_Agent\'>Agent</a></code> (in fact usually this \nwill be a sub-class of <code><a href=\'#term_Person\'>Person</a></code>), and giving criteria \nfor which things fall in or out of the sub-class. For this, we use the \n<code>owl:onProperty</code> and <code>owl:hasValue</code> properties, \nindicating the property/value pairs which must be true of matching \nagents.\n</p>\n\n<div class="example">\n<pre>\n&lt;!-- here we see a FOAF group described.\n     each foaf group may be associated with an OWL definition \n     specifying the class of agents that constitute the group\'s membership --&gt;\n&lt;foaf:Group&gt;\n &lt;foaf:name&gt;ILRT staff&lt;/foaf:name&gt;\n &lt;foaf:membershipClass&gt;\n    &lt;owl:Class rdf:about="http://ilrt.example.com/groups#ILRTStaffPerson"&gt;\n     &lt;rdfs:subClassOf rdf:resource="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Person"/&gt;\n     &lt;rdfs:subClassOf&gt;\n       &lt;owl:Restriction&gt; \n         &lt;owl:onProperty rdf:resource="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/workplaceHomepage"/&gt;\n         &lt;owl:hasValue rdf:resource="http://www.ilrt.bris.ac.uk/"/&gt;\n       &lt;/owl:Restriction&gt; \n     &lt;/rdfs:subClassOf&gt;\n   &lt;/owl:Class&gt;\n &lt;/foaf:membershipClass&gt;\n&lt;/foaf:Group&gt;\n</pre>\n</div>\n  \n\n<p>\nNote that while these example OWL rules for being in the eg:ILRTStaffPerson class are \nbased on a <code><a href=\'#term_Person\'>Person</a></code> having a particular \n<code><a href=\'#term_workplaceHomepage\'>workplaceHomepage</a></code>, this places no obligations on the authors of actual \nFOAF documents to include this information. If the information <em>is</em> included, then \ngeneric OWL tools may infer that some person is an eg:ILRTStaffPerson. To go the extra \nstep and infer that some eg:ILRTStaffPerson is a <code><a href=\'#term_member\'>member</a></code> of the group \nwhose <code><a href=\'#term_name\'>name</a></code> is "ILRT staff", tools will need some knowledge of the way \nFOAF deals with groups. In other words, generic OWL technology gets us most of the way, \nbut the full <code><a href=\'#term_Group\'>Group</a></code> machinery requires extra work for implimentors.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nThe current design names the relationship as pointing <em>from</em> the group, \nto the member. This is convenient when writing XML/RDF that encloses the members within \nmarkup that describes the group. Alternate representations of the same content are \nallowed in RDF, so you can write claims about the Person and the Group without having to \nnest either description inside the other. For (brief) example:\n</p>\n\n<div class="example">\n<pre>\n&lt;foaf:Group&gt;\n &lt;foaf:member rdf:nodeID="martin"/&gt;\n &lt;!-- more about the group here --&gt;\n&lt;/foaf:Group&gt;\n&lt;foaf:Person rdf:nodeID="martin"&gt;\n  &lt;!-- more about martin here --&gt;\n&lt;/foaf:Person&gt;\n</pre>\n</div>\n\n<p>\nThere is a FOAF <a href="http://wiki.foaf-project.org/w/IssueTracker">issue tracker</a> \nassociated with this FOAF term. A design goal is to make the most of W3C\'s <a \nhref="http://www.w3.org/2001/sw/WebOnt">OWL</a> language for representing group-membership \ncriteria, while also making it easy to leverage existing groups and datasets available \nonline (eg. buddylists, mailing list membership lists etc). Feedback on the current design \nis solicited! Should we consider using SPARQL queries instead, for example?\n</p>\n\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_Group">#</a>] <!-- Group Group --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm classterm " id="term_Image" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Image" typeof="rdfs:Class">\n            <h3>Class: foaf:Image</h3> \n            <em>Image</em> - An image. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >stable</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Properties include:</th>\n <td>  <a href="#term_depicts">depicts</a>\n <a href="#term_thumbnail">thumbnail</a>\n </td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Used with:</th>\n <td>  <a href="#term_img">img</a>\n <a href="#term_thumbnail">thumbnail</a>\n <a href="#term_depiction">depiction</a>\n</td></tr> <tr><th>Subclass Of</th>\n <td>  <span rel="rdfs:subClassOf" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Document"><a href="#term_Document">Document</a></span>\n </td></tr>\n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe class <code><a href=\'#term_Image\'>Image</a></code> is a sub-class of <code><a href=\'#term_Document\'>Document</a></code> \ncorresponding to those documents which are images.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nDigital images (such as JPEG, PNG, GIF bitmaps, SVG diagrams etc.) are examples of \n<code><a href=\'#term_Image\'>Image</a></code>. \n</p>\n\n<!-- much more we could/should say here -->\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_Image">#</a>] <!-- Image Image --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm classterm " id="term_Organization" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Organization" typeof="rdfs:Class">\n            <h3>Class: foaf:Organization</h3> \n            <em>Organization</em> - An organization. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >stable</span></td></tr>\n            \n            <tr><th>Subclass Of</th>\n <td>  <span rel="rdfs:subClassOf" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Agent"><a href="#term_Agent">Agent</a></span>\n </td></tr>\n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr><tr><th>Disjoint With:</th>\n <td>  <span rel="owl:disjointWith" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Document"><a href="#term_Document">Document</a></span>\n <span rel="owl:disjointWith" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Person"><a href="#term_Person">Person</a></span>\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_Organization\'>Organization</a></code> class represents a kind of <code><a href=\'#term_Agent\'>Agent</a></code> \ncorresponding to social instititutions such as companies, societies etc.\n</p>\n\n<p class="editorial">\nThis is a more \'solid\' class than <code><a href=\'#term_Group\'>Group</a></code>, which allows \nfor more ad-hoc collections of individuals. These terms, like the corresponding\nnatural language concepts, have some overlap, but different emphasis.\n</p>\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_Organization">#</a>] <!-- Organization Organization --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm classterm " id="term_Person" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Person" typeof="rdfs:Class">\n            <h3>Class: foaf:Person</h3> \n            <em>Person</em> - A person. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >stable</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Properties include:</th>\n <td>  <a href="#term_plan">plan</a>\n <a href="#term_surname">surname</a>\n <a href="#term_geekcode">geekcode</a>\n <a href="#term_pastProject">pastProject</a>\n <a href="#term_lastName">lastName</a>\n <a href="#term_family_name">family_name</a>\n <a href="#term_publications">publications</a>\n <a href="#term_currentProject">currentProject</a>\n <a href="#term_familyName">familyName</a>\n <a href="#term_firstName">firstName</a>\n <a href="#term_workInfoHomepage">workInfoHomepage</a>\n <a href="#term_myersBriggs">myersBriggs</a>\n <a href="#term_schoolHomepage">schoolHomepage</a>\n <a href="#term_img">img</a>\n <a href="#term_workplaceHomepage">workplaceHomepage</a>\n <a href="#term_knows">knows</a>\n </td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Used with:</th>\n <td>  <a href="#term_knows">knows</a>\n</td></tr> <tr><th>Subclass Of</th>\n <td>  <span rel="rdfs:subClassOf" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Agent"><a href="#term_Agent">Agent</a></span>\n <span rel="rdfs:subClassOf" href="http://www.w3.org/2003/01/geo/wgs84_pos#SpatialThing"><a href="#term_SpatialThing">Spatial Thing</a></span>\n </td></tr>\n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr><tr><th>Disjoint With:</th>\n <td>  <span rel="owl:disjointWith" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Project"><a href="#term_Project">Project</a></span>\n <span rel="owl:disjointWith" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Organization"><a href="#term_Organization">Organization</a></span>\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_Person\'>Person</a></code> class represents people. Something is a \n<code><a href=\'#term_Person\'>Person</a></code> if it is a person. We don\'t nitpic about whether they\'re \nalive, dead, real, or imaginary. The <code><a href=\'#term_Person\'>Person</a></code> class is a sub-class of the \n<code><a href=\'#term_Agent\'>Agent</a></code> class, since all people are considered \'agents\' in FOAF. \n</p>\n\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_Person">#</a>] <!-- Person Person --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div>\n<div class="specterm classterm " id="term_OnlineAccount" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/OnlineAccount" typeof="rdfs:Class">\n            <h3>Class: foaf:OnlineAccount</h3> \n            <em>Online Account</em> - An online account. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >testing</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Properties include:</th>\n <td>  <a href="#term_accountName">accountName</a>\n <a href="#term_accountServiceHomepage">accountServiceHomepage</a>\n </td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Used with:</th>\n <td>  <a href="#term_account">account</a>\n <a href="#term_holdsAccount">holdsAccount</a>\n</td></tr> <tr><th>Subclass Of</th>\n <td>  <span rel="rdfs:subClassOf" href="http://www.w3.org/2002/07/owl#Thing"><a href="#term_Thing">Thing</a></span>\n </td></tr><tr><th>Has Subclass</th>\n <td>  <a href="#term_OnlineEcommerceAccount">Online E-commerce Account</a>\n <a href="#term_OnlineGamingAccount">Online Gaming Account</a>\n <a href="#term_OnlineChatAccount">Online Chat Account</a>\n </td></tr>\n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_OnlineAccount\'>OnlineAccount</a></code> class represents the provision of some form of online \nservice, by some party (indicated indirectly via a <code><a href=\'#term_accountServiceHomepage\'>accountServiceHomepage</a></code>) to some <code><a href=\'#term_Agent\'>Agent</a></code>. The \n<code><a href=\'#term_account\'>account</a></code> property of the agent is used to indicate accounts that are associated with the agent.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nSee <code><a href=\'#term_OnlineChatAccount\'>OnlineChatAccount</a></code> for an example. Other sub-classes include <code><a href=\'#term_OnlineEcommerceAccount\'>OnlineEcommerceAccount</a></code> and <code><a href=\'#term_OnlineGamingAccount\'>OnlineGamingAccount</a></code>.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nOne deployment style for this construct is to use URIs for well-known documents (or other entities) that strongly embody the account-holding relationship; for \nexample, user profile pages on social network sites. This has the advantage of providing URIs that are likely to be easy to link with other information,\nbut means that the instances of this class should not be considered \'accounts\' in the abstract or business sense of a \'contract\'.\n</p>\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_OnlineAccount">#</a>] <!-- OnlineAccount OnlineAccount --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm classterm " id="term_PersonalProfileDocument" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/PersonalProfileDocument" typeof="rdfs:Class">\n            <h3>Class: foaf:PersonalProfileDocument</h3> \n            <em>PersonalProfileDocument</em> - A personal profile RDF document. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >testing</span></td></tr>\n            \n            <tr><th>Subclass Of</th>\n <td>  <span rel="rdfs:subClassOf" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Document"><a href="#term_Document">Document</a></span>\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_PersonalProfileDocument\'>PersonalProfileDocument</a></code> class represents those\nthings that are a <code><a href=\'#term_Document\'>Document</a></code>, and that use RDF to\ndescribe properties of the person who is the <code><a href=\'#term_maker\'>maker</a></code>\nof the document. There is just one <code><a href=\'#term_Person\'>Person</a></code> described in\nthe document, ie.\nthe person who <code><a href=\'#term_made\'>made</a></code> it and who will be its\n<code><a href=\'#term_primaryTopic\'>primaryTopic</a></code>.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_PersonalProfileDocument\'>PersonalProfileDocument</a></code> class, and FOAF\'s\nassociated conventions for describing it, captures an important\ndeployment pattern for the FOAF vocabulary. FOAF is very often used in\npublic RDF documents made available through the Web. There is a\ncolloquial notion that these "FOAF files" are often <em>somebody\'s</em>\nFOAF file. Through <code><a href=\'#term_PersonalProfileDocument\'>PersonalProfileDocument</a></code> we provide\na machine-readable expression of this concept, providing a basis for \nFOAF documents to make claims about their maker and topic.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nWhen describing a <code><a href=\'#term_PersonalProfileDocument\'>PersonalProfileDocument</a></code> it is\ntypical (and useful) to describe its associated <code><a href=\'#term_Person\'>Person</a></code>\nusing the <code><a href=\'#term_maker\'>maker</a></code> property. Anything that is a\n<code><a href=\'#term_Person\'>Person</a></code> and that is the <code><a href=\'#term_maker\'>maker</a></code> of some\n<code><a href=\'#term_PersonalProfileDocument\'>PersonalProfileDocument</a></code> will be the <code><a href=\'#term_primaryTopic\'>primaryTopic</a></code> of\nthat <code><a href=\'#term_Document\'>Document</a></code>. Although this can be inferred, it is\noften helpful to include this information explicitly within the\n<code><a href=\'#term_PersonalProfileDocument\'>PersonalProfileDocument</a></code>.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nFor example, here is a fragment of a personal profile document which\ndescribes its author explicitly:\n</p>\n<div class="example">\n<pre>\n&lt;foaf:Person rdf:nodeID="p1"&gt;\n &lt;foaf:name&gt;Dan Brickley&lt;/foaf:name&gt;\n &lt;foaf:homepage rdf:resource="http://danbri.org/"/&gt;\n &lt;!-- etc... --&gt;\n&lt;/foaf:Person&gt;\n\n&lt;foaf:PersonalProfileDocument rdf:about=""&gt;\n   &lt;foaf:maker rdf:nodeID="p1"/&gt;\n   &lt;foaf:primaryTopic rdf:nodeID="p1"/&gt;\n&lt;/foaf:PersonalProfileDocument&gt;\n</pre>\n</div>\n\n<p>\nNote that a <code><a href=\'#term_PersonalProfileDocument\'>PersonalProfileDocument</a></code> will have some\nrepresentation as RDF. Typically this will be in W3C\'s RDF/XML syntax,\nhowever we leave open the possibility for the use of other notations, or\nrepresentational conventions including automated transformations from\nHTML (<a href="http://www.w3.org/2004/01/rdxh/spec">GRDDL</a> spec for\none such technique).\n</p>\n\n\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_PersonalProfileDocument">#</a>] <!-- PersonalProfileDocument PersonalProfileDocument --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm classterm " id="term_Project" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Project" typeof="rdfs:Class">\n            <h3>Class: foaf:Project</h3> \n            <em>Project</em> - A project (a collective endeavour of some kind). <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >testing</span></td></tr>\n            \n            \n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr><tr><th>Disjoint With:</th>\n <td>  <span rel="owl:disjointWith" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Document"><a href="#term_Document">Document</a></span>\n <span rel="owl:disjointWith" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Person"><a href="#term_Person">Person</a></span>\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_Project\'>Project</a></code> class represents the class of things that are \'projects\'. These \nmay be formal or informal, collective or individual. It is often useful to indicate the \n<code><a href=\'#term_homepage\'>homepage</a></code> of a <code><a href=\'#term_Project\'>Project</a></code>.\n</p>\n\n<p class="editorial">\nFurther work is needed to specify the connections between this class and the FOAF properties \n<code><a href=\'#term_currentProject\'>currentProject</a></code> and <code><a href=\'#term_pastProject\'>pastProject</a></code>.\n</p>\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_Project">#</a>] <!-- Project Project --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div>\n<div class="specterm classterm " id="term_LabelProperty" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/LabelProperty" typeof="rdfs:Class">\n            <h3>Class: foaf:LabelProperty</h3> \n            <em>Label Property</em> - A foaf:LabelProperty is any RDF property with texual values that serve as labels. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >unstable</span></td></tr>\n            \n            \n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nA <code><a href=\'#term_LabelProperty\'>LabelProperty</a></code> is any RDF property with texual values that serve as labels.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nAny property that is a <code><a href=\'#term_LabelProperty\'>LabelProperty</a></code> is effectively a sub-property of rdfs:label. This utility\nclass provides an alternate means of expressing this idea, in a way that may help with OWL 2.0 DL compatibility.\n</p>\n\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_LabelProperty">#</a>] <!-- LabelProperty LabelProperty --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm classterm " id="term_OnlineChatAccount" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/OnlineChatAccount" typeof="rdfs:Class">\n            <h3>Class: foaf:OnlineChatAccount</h3> \n            <em>Online Chat Account</em> - An online chat account. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >unstable</span></td></tr>\n            \n            <tr><th>Subclass Of</th>\n <td>  <span rel="rdfs:subClassOf" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/OnlineAccount"><a href="#term_OnlineAccount">Online Account</a></span>\n </td></tr>\n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nA <code><a href=\'#term_OnlineChatAccount\'>OnlineChatAccount</a></code> is a <code><a href=\'#term_OnlineAccount\'>OnlineAccount</a></code> devoted to \nchat / instant messaging. The account may offer other services too; FOAF\'s \nsub-classes of <code><a href=\'#term_OnlineAccount\'>OnlineAccount</a></code> are not mutually disjoint.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nThis is a generalization of the FOAF Chat ID properties, \n<code><a href=\'#term_jabberID\'>jabberID</a></code>, <code><a href=\'#term_aimChatID\'>aimChatID</a></code>, \n<code><a href=\'#term_skypeID\'>skypeID</a></code>, <code><a href=\'#term_msnChatID\'>msnChatID</a></code>, <code><a href=\'#term_icqChatID\'>icqChatID</a></code> and \n<code><a href=\'#term_yahooChatID\'>yahooChatID</a></code>. \n</p>\n\n<p>\nUnlike those simple properties, <code><a href=\'#term_OnlineAccount\'>OnlineAccount</a></code> and associated FOAF terms \nallows us to describe a great variety of online accounts, without having to anticipate \nthem in the FOAF vocabulary.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nFor example, here is a description of an IRC chat account, specific to the Freenode IRC \nnetwork:\n</p>\n\n<div class="example">\n<pre>\n&lt;foaf:Person&gt;\n  &lt;foaf:name&gt;Dan Brickley&lt;/foaf:name&gt;\n  &lt;foaf:account&gt;\n    &lt;foaf:OnlineAccount&gt;\n      &lt;rdf:type rdf:resource="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/OnlineChatAccount"/&gt;\n      &lt;foaf:accountServiceHomepage \n               rdf:resource="http://www.freenode.net/"/&gt;\n      &lt;foaf:accountName&gt;danbri&lt;/foaf:accountName&gt;\n    &lt;/foaf:OnlineAccount&gt;\n  &lt;/foaf:account&gt;\n&lt;/foaf:Person&gt;\n</pre>\n</div>\n\n<p>\nNote that it may be impolite to carelessly reveal someone else\'s chat identifier (which \nmight also serve as an indicate of email address) As with email,  there are privacy and \nanti-SPAM considerations. FOAF does not currently provide a way to  represent an \nobfuscated chat ID (ie. there is no parallel to the <code><a href=\'#term_mbox\'>mbox</a></code> / \n<code><a href=\'#term_mbox_sha1sum\'>mbox_sha1sum</a></code> mapping). \n</p>\n<p>\nIn addition to the generic <code><a href=\'#term_OnlineAccount\'>OnlineAccount</a></code> and \n<code><a href=\'#term_OnlineChatAccount\'>OnlineChatAccount</a></code> mechanisms, \nFOAF also provides several convenience chat ID properties \n(<code><a href=\'#term_jabberID\'>jabberID</a></code>, <code><a href=\'#term_aimChatID\'>aimChatID</a></code>, <code><a href=\'#term_icqChatID\'>icqChatID</a></code>, \n<code><a href=\'#term_msnChatID\'>msnChatID</a></code>,<code><a href=\'#term_yahooChatID\'>yahooChatID</a></code>, <code><a href=\'#term_skypeID\'>skypeID</a></code>). \nThese serve as as a shorthand for some common cases; their use may not always be \nappropriate.\n</p>\n<p class="editorial">\nWe should specify some mappings between the abbreviated and full representations of \n<a href="http://www.jabber.org/">Jabber</a>, <a href="http://www.aim.com/">AIM</a>, <a \nhref="http://chat.msn.com/">MSN</a>, <a href="http://web.icq.com/icqchat/">ICQ</a>, <a \nhref="http://chat.yahoo.com/">Yahoo!</a> and <a href="http://chat.msn.com/">MSN</a> chat \naccounts. This has been done for <code><a href=\'#term_skypeID\'>skypeID</a></code>. This requires us to \nidentify an appropriate <code><a href=\'#term_accountServiceHomepage\'>accountServiceHomepage</a></code> for each. If we \nwanted to make the <code><a href=\'#term_OnlineAccount\'>OnlineAccount</a></code> mechanism even more generic, we could \ninvent a relationship that holds between a <code><a href=\'#term_OnlineAccount\'>OnlineAccount</a></code> instance and a \nconvenience property. To continue the example above, we could describe how <a \nhref="http://www.freenode.net/">Freenode</a> could define a property \'fn:freenodeChatID\' \ncorresponding to Freenode online accounts.\n</p>\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_OnlineChatAccount">#</a>] <!-- OnlineChatAccount OnlineChatAccount --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm classterm " id="term_OnlineEcommerceAccount" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/OnlineEcommerceAccount" typeof="rdfs:Class">\n            <h3>Class: foaf:OnlineEcommerceAccount</h3> \n            <em>Online E-commerce Account</em> - An online e-commerce account. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >unstable</span></td></tr>\n            \n            <tr><th>Subclass Of</th>\n <td>  <span rel="rdfs:subClassOf" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/OnlineAccount"><a href="#term_OnlineAccount">Online Account</a></span>\n </td></tr>\n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nA <code><a href=\'#term_OnlineEcommerceAccount\'>OnlineEcommerceAccount</a></code> is a <code><a href=\'#term_OnlineAccount\'>OnlineAccount</a></code> devoted to \nbuying and/or selling of goods, services etc. Examples include <a \nhref="http://www.amazon.com/">Amazon</a>, <a href="http://www.ebay.com/">eBay</a>, <a \nhref="http://www.paypal.com/">PayPal</a>, <a \nhref="http://www.thinkgeek.com/">thinkgeek</a>, etc.\n</p>\n\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_OnlineEcommerceAccount">#</a>] <!-- OnlineEcommerceAccount OnlineEcommerceAccount --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm classterm " id="term_OnlineGamingAccount" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/OnlineGamingAccount" typeof="rdfs:Class">\n            <h3>Class: foaf:OnlineGamingAccount</h3> \n            <em>Online Gaming Account</em> - An online gaming account. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >unstable</span></td></tr>\n            \n            <tr><th>Subclass Of</th>\n <td>  <span rel="rdfs:subClassOf" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/OnlineAccount"><a href="#term_OnlineAccount">Online Account</a></span>\n </td></tr>\n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nA <code><a href=\'#term_OnlineGamingAccount\'>OnlineGamingAccount</a></code> is a <code><a href=\'#term_OnlineAccount\'>OnlineAccount</a></code> devoted to \nonline gaming.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nExamples might include <a href="http://everquest.station.sony.com/">EverQuest</a>, \n<a href="http://www.xbox.com/live/">Xbox live</a>, <a \nhref="http://nwn.bioware.com/">Neverwinter Nights</a>, etc., as well as older text-based \nsystems (MOOs, MUDs and suchlike).\n</p>\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_OnlineGamingAccount">#</a>] <!-- OnlineGamingAccount OnlineGamingAccount --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div>\n<h2>Properties</h2>\n <div class="specterm propertyterm " id="term_homepage" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/homepage" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:homepage</h3> \n            <em>homepage</em> - A homepage for some thing. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >stable</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://www.w3.org/2002/07/owl#Thing"><a href="#term_Thing">Thing</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Range:</th>\n <td> every value of this property is a  <span rel="rdfs:range" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Document"><a href="#term_Document">Document</a></span>\n</td>\t</tr>\n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr><tr><th colspan="2">Inverse Functional Property</th>\n <td> <span rel="rdf:type" href="http://www.w3.org/2002/07/owl#InverseFunctionalProperty"></span> </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_homepage\'>homepage</a></code> property relates something to a homepage about it.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nMany kinds of things have homepages. FOAF allows a thing to have multiple homepages, but \nconstrains <code><a href=\'#term_homepage\'>homepage</a></code> so that there can be only one thing that has any \nparticular homepage.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nA \'homepage\' in this sense is a public Web document, typically but not necessarily \navailable in HTML format. The page has as a <code><a href=\'#term_topic\'>topic</a></code> the thing whose \nhomepage it is. The homepage is usually controlled, edited or published by the thing whose \nhomepage it is; as such one might look to a homepage for information on its owner from its \nowner. This works for people, companies, organisations etc. \n</p>\n\n<p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_homepage\'>homepage</a></code> property is a sub-property of the more general \n<code><a href=\'#term_page\'>page</a></code> property for relating a thing to a page about that thing. See also \n<code><a href=\'#term_topic\'>topic</a></code>, the inverse of the <code><a href=\'#term_page\'>page</a></code> property. \n</p>\n\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_homepage">#</a>] <!-- homepage homepage --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm " id="term_isPrimaryTopicOf" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/isPrimaryTopicOf" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:isPrimaryTopicOf</h3> \n            <em>is primary topic of</em> - A document that this thing is the primary topic of. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >stable</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://www.w3.org/2002/07/owl#Thing"><a href="#term_Thing">Thing</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Range:</th>\n <td> every value of this property is a  <span rel="rdfs:range" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Document"><a href="#term_Document">Document</a></span>\n</td>\t</tr>\n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr><tr><th colspan="2">Inverse Functional Property</th>\n <td> <span rel="rdf:type" href="http://www.w3.org/2002/07/owl#InverseFunctionalProperty"></span> </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_isPrimaryTopicOf\'>isPrimaryTopicOf</a></code> property relates something to a document that is \nmainly about it.\n</p>\n\n\n<p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_isPrimaryTopicOf\'>isPrimaryTopicOf</a></code> property is <em>inverse functional</em>: for\nany document that is the value of this property, there is at most one thing in the world\nthat is the primary topic of that document. This is useful, as it allows for data \nmerging, as described in the documentation for its inverse, <code><a href=\'#term_primaryTopic\'>primaryTopic</a></code>.\n</p>\n\n<p>\n<code><a href=\'#term_page\'>page</a></code> is a super-property of <code><a href=\'#term_isPrimaryTopicOf\'>isPrimaryTopicOf</a></code>. The change \nof terminology between the two property names reflects the utility of \'primaryTopic\' and its \ninverse when identifying things. Anything that has an <code>isPrimaryTopicOf</code> relation \nto some document X, also has a <code><a href=\'#term_page\'>page</a></code> relationship to it.\n</p>\n<p>\nNote that <code><a href=\'#term_homepage\'>homepage</a></code>, is a sub-property of both <code><a href=\'#term_page\'>page</a></code> and \n<code><a href=\'#term_isPrimaryTopicOf\'>isPrimaryTopicOf</a></code>. The awkwardly named \n<code><a href=\'#term_isPrimaryTopicOf\'>isPrimaryTopicOf</a></code> is less specific, and can be used with any document \nthat is primarily about the thing of interest (ie. not just on homepages).\n</p>\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_isPrimaryTopicOf">#</a>] <!-- isPrimaryTopicOf isPrimaryTopicOf --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm " id="term_knows" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/knows" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:knows</h3> \n            <em>knows</em> - A person known by this person (indicating some level of reciprocated interaction between the parties). <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >stable</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Person"><a href="#term_Person">Person</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Range:</th>\n <td> every value of this property is a  <span rel="rdfs:range" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Person"><a href="#term_Person">Person</a></span>\n</td>\t</tr>\n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_knows\'>knows</a></code> property relates a <code><a href=\'#term_Person\'>Person</a></code> to another \n<code><a href=\'#term_Person\'>Person</a></code> that he or she knows. \n</p>\n\n<p>\nWe take a broad view of \'knows\', but do require some form of reciprocated interaction (ie. \nstalkers need not apply). Since social attitudes and conventions on this topic vary \ngreatly between communities, counties and cultures, it is not appropriate for FOAF to be \noverly-specific here. \n</p>\n\n<p>\nIf someone <code><a href=\'#term_knows\'>knows</a></code> a person, it would be usual for \nthe relation to be reciprocated. However this doesn\'t mean that there is any obligation \nfor either party to publish FOAF describing this relationship. A <code><a href=\'#term_knows\'>knows</a></code> \nrelationship does not imply friendship, endorsement, or that a face-to-face meeting \nhas taken place: phone, fax, email, and smoke signals are all perfectly \nacceptable ways of communicating with people you know. \n</p>\n<p>\nYou probably know hundreds of people, yet might only list a few in your public FOAF file. \nThat\'s OK. Or you might list them all. It is perfectly fine to have a FOAF file and not \nlist anyone else in it at all. \nThis illustrates the Semantic Web principle of partial description: RDF documents \nrarely describe the entire picture. There is always more to be said, more information \nliving elsewhere in the Web (or in our heads...).\n</p>\n\n<p>\nSince <code><a href=\'#term_knows\'>knows</a></code> is vague by design, it may be suprising that it has uses. \nTypically these involve combining other RDF properties. For example, an application might \nlook at properties of each <code><a href=\'#term_weblog\'>weblog</a></code> that was <code><a href=\'#term_made\'>made</a></code> by \nsomeone you "<code><a href=\'#term_knows\'>knows</a></code>". Or check the newsfeed of the online photo archive \nfor each of these people, to show you recent photos taken by people you know.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nTo provide additional levels of representation beyond mere \'knows\', FOAF applications \ncan do several things. \n</p>\n<p>\nThey can use more precise relationships than <code><a href=\'#term_knows\'>knows</a></code> to relate people to \npeople. The original FOAF design included two of these (\'knowsWell\',\'friend\') which we \nremoved because they were somewhat <em>awkward</em> to actually use, bringing an \ninappopriate air of precision to an intrinsically vague concept. Other extensions have \nbeen proposed, including Eric Vitiello\'s <a \nhref="http://www.perceive.net/schemas/20021119/relationship/">Relationship module</a> for \nFOAF.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nIn addition to using more specialised inter-personal relationship types \n(eg rel:acquaintanceOf etc) it is often just as good to use RDF descriptions of the states \nof affairs which imply particular kinds of relationship. So for example, two people who \nhave the same value for their <code><a href=\'#term_workplaceHomepage\'>workplaceHomepage</a></code> property are \ntypically colleagues. We don\'t (currently) clutter FOAF up with these extra relationships, \nbut the facts can be written in FOAF nevertheless. Similarly, if there exists a \n<code><a href=\'#term_Document\'>Document</a></code> that has two people listed as its <code><a href=\'#term_maker\'>maker</a></code>s, \nthen they are probably collaborators of some kind. Or if two people appear in 100s of \ndigital photos together, there\'s a good chance they\'re friends and/or colleagues.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nSo FOAF is quite pluralistic in its approach to representing relationships between people. \nFOAF is built on top of a general purpose machine language for representing relationships \n(ie. RDF), so is quite capable of representing any kinds of relationship we care to add. \nThe problems are generally social rather than technical; deciding on appropriate ways of \ndescribing these interconnections is a subtle art.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nPerhaps the most important use of <code><a href=\'#term_knows\'>knows</a></code> is, alongside the \n<code>rdfs:seeAlso</code> property, to connect FOAF files together. Taken alone, a FOAF \nfile is somewhat dull. But linked in with 1000s of other FOAF files it becomes more \ninteresting, with each FOAF file saying a little more about people, places, documents, things... \nBy mentioning other people (via <code><a href=\'#term_knows\'>knows</a></code> or other relationships), and by \nproviding an <code>rdfs:seeAlso</code> link to their FOAF file, you can make it easy for \nFOAF indexing tools (\'<a href="http://wiki.foaf-project.org/w/ScutterSpec">scutters</a>\') to find \nyour FOAF and the FOAF of the people you\'ve mentioned. And the FOAF of the people they \nmention, and so on. This makes it possible to build FOAF aggregators without the need for \na centrally managed directory of FOAF files...\n</p>\n\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_knows">#</a>] <!-- knows knows --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm " id="term_made" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/made" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:made</h3> \n            <em>made</em> - Something that was made by this agent. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >stable</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Agent"><a href="#term_Agent">Agent</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Range:</th>\n <td> every value of this property is a  <span rel="rdfs:range" href="http://www.w3.org/2002/07/owl#Thing"><a href="#term_Thing">Thing</a></span>\n</td>\t</tr>\n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_made\'>made</a></code> property relates a <code><a href=\'#term_Agent\'>Agent</a></code> \nto something <code><a href=\'#term_made\'>made</a></code> by it. As such it is an \ninverse of the <code><a href=\'#term_maker\'>maker</a></code> property, which relates a thing to \nsomething that made it. See <code><a href=\'#term_made\'>made</a></code> for more details on the \nrelationship between these FOAF terms and related Dublin Core vocabulary.\n</p>\n\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_made">#</a>] <!-- made made --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm " id="term_maker" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/maker" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:maker</h3> \n            <em>maker</em> - An agent that  made this thing. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >stable</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://www.w3.org/2002/07/owl#Thing"><a href="#term_Thing">Thing</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Range:</th>\n <td> every value of this property is a  <span rel="rdfs:range" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Agent"><a href="#term_Agent">Agent</a></span>\n</td>\t</tr>\n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_maker\'>maker</a></code> property relates something to a  \n<code><a href=\'#term_Agent\'>Agent</a></code> that <code><a href=\'#term_made\'>made</a></code> it. As such it is an \ninverse of the <code><a href=\'#term_made\'>made</a></code> property.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_name\'>name</a></code> (or other <code>rdfs:label</code>) of the \n<code><a href=\'#term_maker\'>maker</a></code> of something can be described as the \n<code>dc:creator</code> of that thing.</p>\n\n<p>\nFor example, if the thing named by the URI \nhttp://danbri.org/ has a \n<code><a href=\'#term_maker\'>maker</a></code> that is a <code><a href=\'#term_Person\'>Person</a></code> whose \n<code><a href=\'#term_name\'>name</a></code> is \'Dan Brickley\', we can conclude that \nhttp://danbri.org/ has a <code>dc:creator</code> of \'Dan \nBrickley\'. \n</p>\n\n<p>\nFOAF descriptions are encouraged to use <code>dc:creator</code> only for \nsimple textual names, and to use <code><a href=\'#term_maker\'>maker</a></code> to indicate \ncreators, rather than risk confusing creators with their names. This \nfollows most Dublin Core usage. See <a \nhref="http://wiki.foaf-project.org/w/UsingDublinCoreCreator">UsingDublinCoreCreator</a> \nfor details.\n</p>\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_maker">#</a>] <!-- maker maker --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm " id="term_mbox" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/mbox" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:mbox</h3> \n            <em>personal mailbox</em> - A  personal mailbox, ie. an Internet mailbox associated with exactly one owner, the first owner of this mailbox. This is a \'static inverse functional property\', in that  there is (across time and change) at most one individual that ever has any particular value for foaf:mbox. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >stable</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Agent"><a href="#term_Agent">Agent</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Range:</th>\n <td> every value of this property is a  <span rel="rdfs:range" href="http://www.w3.org/2002/07/owl#Thing"><a href="#term_Thing">Thing</a></span>\n</td>\t</tr>\n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr><tr><th colspan="2">Inverse Functional Property</th>\n <td> <span rel="rdf:type" href="http://www.w3.org/2002/07/owl#InverseFunctionalProperty"></span> </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_mbox\'>mbox</a></code> property is a relationship between the owner of a mailbox and \na mailbox. These are typically identified using the mailto: URI scheme (see <a \nhref="http://ftp.ics.uci.edu/pub/ietf/uri/rfc2368.txt">RFC 2368</a>).\n</p>\n\n<p>\nNote that there are many mailboxes (eg. shared ones) which are not the \n<code><a href=\'#term_mbox\'>mbox</a></code> of anyone. Furthermore, a person can have multiple \n<code><a href=\'#term_mbox\'>mbox</a></code> properties. \n</p>\n\n<p>\nIn FOAF, we often see <code><a href=\'#term_mbox\'>mbox</a></code> used as an indirect way of identifying its \nowner. This works even if the mailbox is itself out of service (eg. 10 years old), since \nthe property is defined in terms of its primary owner, and doesn\'t require the mailbox to \nactually be being used for anything.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nMany people are wary of sharing information about their mailbox addresses in public. To \naddress such concerns whilst continuing the FOAF convention of indirectly identifying \npeople by referring to widely known properties, FOAF also provides the \n<code><a href=\'#term_mbox_sha1sum\'>mbox_sha1sum</a></code> mechanism, which is a relationship between a person and \nthe value you get from passing a mailbox URI to the SHA1 mathematical function.\n</p>\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_mbox">#</a>] <!-- mbox mbox --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm " id="term_member" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/member" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:member</h3> \n            <em>member</em> - Indicates a member of a Group <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >stable</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Group"><a href="#term_Group">Group</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Range:</th>\n <td> every value of this property is a  <span rel="rdfs:range" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Agent"><a href="#term_Agent">Agent</a></span>\n</td>\t</tr>\n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_member\'>member</a></code> property relates a <code><a href=\'#term_Group\'>Group</a></code> to a \n<code><a href=\'#term_Agent\'>Agent</a></code> that is a member of that group.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nSee <code><a href=\'#term_Group\'>Group</a></code> for details and examples.\n</p>\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_member">#</a>] <!-- member member --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm " id="term_page" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/page" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:page</h3> \n            <em>page</em> - A page or document about this thing. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >stable</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://www.w3.org/2002/07/owl#Thing"><a href="#term_Thing">Thing</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Range:</th>\n <td> every value of this property is a  <span rel="rdfs:range" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Document"><a href="#term_Document">Document</a></span>\n</td>\t</tr>\n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_page\'>page</a></code> property relates a thing to a document about that thing.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nAs such it is an inverse of the <code><a href=\'#term_topic\'>topic</a></code> property, which relates a document \nto a thing that the document is about.\n</p>\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_page">#</a>] <!-- page page --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm " id="term_primaryTopic" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/primaryTopic" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:primaryTopic</h3> \n            <em>primary topic</em> - The primary topic of some page or document. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >stable</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Document"><a href="#term_Document">Document</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Range:</th>\n <td> every value of this property is a  <span rel="rdfs:range" href="http://www.w3.org/2002/07/owl#Thing"><a href="#term_Thing">Thing</a></span>\n</td>\t</tr>\n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr><tr><th colspan="2">Functional Property</th>\n <td> <span rel="rdf:type" href="http://www.w3.org/2002/07/owl#FunctionalProperty"></span> </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_primaryTopic\'>primaryTopic</a></code> property relates a document to the\nmain thing that the document is about.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_primaryTopic\'>primaryTopic</a></code> property is <em>functional</em>: for\nany document it applies to, it can have at most one value. This is\nuseful, as it allows for data merging. In many cases it may be difficult\nfor third parties to determine the primary topic of a document, but in\na useful number of cases (eg. descriptions of movies, restaurants,\npoliticians, ...) it should be reasonably obvious. Documents are very\noften the most authoritative source of information about their own\nprimary topics, although this cannot be guaranteed since documents cannot be\nassumed to be accurate, honest etc.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nIt is an inverse of the <code><a href=\'#term_isPrimaryTopicOf\'>isPrimaryTopicOf</a></code> property, which relates a \nthing to  a document <em>primarily</em> about that thing. The choice between these two \nproperties is purely pragmatic. When describing documents, we \nuse <code><a href=\'#term_primaryTopic\'>primaryTopic</a></code> former to point to the things they\'re about. When \ndescribing things (people etc.), it is useful to be able to directly cite documents which \nhave those things as their main topic - so we use <code><a href=\'#term_isPrimaryTopicOf\'>isPrimaryTopicOf</a></code>. In this \nway, Web sites such as <a href="http://www.wikipedia.org/">Wikipedia</a> or <a \nhref="http://www.nndb.com/">NNDB</a> can provide indirect identification for the things they \nhave descriptions of.\n</p>\n\n\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_primaryTopic">#</a>] <!-- primaryTopic primaryTopic --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm " id="term_weblog" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/weblog" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:weblog</h3> \n            <em>weblog</em> - A weblog of some thing (whether person, group, company etc.). <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >stable</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Agent"><a href="#term_Agent">Agent</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Range:</th>\n <td> every value of this property is a  <span rel="rdfs:range" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Document"><a href="#term_Document">Document</a></span>\n</td>\t</tr>\n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr><tr><th colspan="2">Inverse Functional Property</th>\n <td> <span rel="rdf:type" href="http://www.w3.org/2002/07/owl#InverseFunctionalProperty"></span> </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_weblog\'>weblog</a></code> property relates a <code><a href=\'#term_Agent\'>Agent</a></code> to a weblog of \nthat agent.\n</p>\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_weblog">#</a>] <!-- weblog weblog --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div>\n<div class="specterm propertyterm " id="term_account" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/account" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:account</h3> \n            <em>account</em> - Indicates an account held by this agent. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >testing</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Agent"><a href="#term_Agent">Agent</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Range:</th>\n <td> every value of this property is a  <span rel="rdfs:range" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/OnlineAccount"><a href="#term_OnlineAccount">Online Account</a></span>\n</td>\t</tr>\n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_account\'>account</a></code> property relates a <code><a href=\'#term_Agent\'>Agent</a></code> to an \n<code><a href=\'#term_OnlineAccount\'>OnlineAccount</a></code> for which they are the sole account holder. See \n<code><a href=\'#term_OnlineAccount\'>OnlineAccount</a></code> for usage details.\n</p>\n\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_account">#</a>] <!-- account account --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm " id="term_accountName" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/accountName" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:accountName</h3> \n            <em>account name</em> - Indicates the name (identifier) associated with this online account. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >testing</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/OnlineAccount"><a href="#term_OnlineAccount">Online Account</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            \n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_accountName\'>accountName</a></code> property of a <code><a href=\'#term_OnlineAccount\'>OnlineAccount</a></code> is a \ntextual representation of the account name (unique ID) associated with that account.\n</p>\n\n\n            <p style="float: right;
