}">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >testing</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/OnlineAccount"><a href="#term_OnlineAccount">Online Account</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Range:</th>\n <td> every value of this property is a  <span rel="rdfs:range" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Document"><a href="#term_Document">Document</a></span>\n</td>\t</tr>\n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_accountServiceHomepage\'>accountServiceHomepage</a></code> property indicates a relationship between a \n<code><a href=\'#term_OnlineAccount\'>OnlineAccount</a></code> and the homepage of the supporting service provider.\n</p>\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_accountServiceHomepage">#</a>] <!-- accountServiceHomepage accountServiceHomepage --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm " id="term_aimChatID" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/aimChatID" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:aimChatID</h3> \n            <em>AIM chat ID</em> - An AIM chat ID <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >testing</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Agent"><a href="#term_Agent">Agent</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            \n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr><tr><th colspan="2">Inverse Functional Property</th>\n <td> <span rel="rdf:type" href="http://www.w3.org/2002/07/owl#InverseFunctionalProperty"></span> </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_aimChatID\'>aimChatID</a></code> property relates a <code><a href=\'#term_Agent\'>Agent</a></code> to a textual \nidentifier (\'screenname\') assigned to them in the AOL Instant Messanger (AIM) system. \nSee AOL\'s <a href="http://www.aim.com/">AIM</a> site for more details of AIM and AIM \nscreennames. The <a href="http://www.apple.com/macosx/what-is-macosx/ichat.html">iChat</a> tools from <a \nhref="http://www.apple.com/">Apple</a> also make use of AIM identifiers. \n</p>\n\n<p>\nSee <code><a href=\'#term_OnlineChatAccount\'>OnlineChatAccount</a></code> (and <code><a href=\'#term_OnlineAccount\'>OnlineAccount</a></code>) for a\nmore general (and verbose) mechanism for describing IM and chat accounts.\n</p>\n\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_aimChatID">#</a>] <!-- aimChatID aimChatID --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm " id="term_based_near" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/based_near" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:based_near</h3> \n            <em>based near</em> - A location that something is based near, for some broadly human notion of near. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >testing</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://www.w3.org/2003/01/geo/wgs84_pos#SpatialThing"><a href="#term_SpatialThing">Spatial Thing</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Range:</th>\n <td> every value of this property is a  <span rel="rdfs:range" href="http://www.w3.org/2003/01/geo/wgs84_pos#SpatialThing"><a href="#term_SpatialThing">Spatial Thing</a></span>\n</td>\t</tr>\n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <!-- note: is this mis-specified? perhaps range ought to also be SpatialThing, so we\n     can have multiple labels on the same point? -->\n\n<p>The <code><a href=\'#term_based_near\'>based_near</a></code> relationship relates two "spatial \nthings"\n(anything that can <em>be somewhere</em>), the latter typically\ndescribed using the geo:lat / geo:long\n<a href="http://www.w3.org/2003/01/geo/wgs84_pos#">geo-positioning vocabulary</a>\n(See <a href="http://esw.w3.org/topic/GeoInfo">GeoInfo</a> in the W3C semweb\nwiki for details). This allows us to say describe the typical latitute and \nlongitude of, say, a Person (people are spatial things - they can be \nplaces) without implying that a precise location has been given.\n</p>\n\n<p>We do not say much about what \'near\' means in this context; it is a\n\'rough and ready\' concept. For a more precise treatment, see\n<a href="http://esw.w3.org/topic/GeoOnion">GeoOnion vocab</a> design\ndiscussions, which are aiming to produce a more sophisticated vocabulary for \nsuch purposes.\n</p>\n\n<p> FOAF files often make use of the <code>contact:nearestAirport</code> property. This\nillustrates the distinction between FOAF documents (which may make claims using <em>any</em> RDF\nvocabulary) and the core FOAF vocabulary defined by this specification. For further reading on\nthe use of <code>nearestAirport</code> see <a \nhref="http://wiki.foaf-project.org/w/UsingContactNearestAirport">UsingContactNearestAirport</a> in the \nFOAF wiki.\n</p>\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_based_near">#</a>] <!-- based_near based_near --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm " id="term_currentProject" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/currentProject" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:currentProject</h3> \n            <em>current project</em> - A current project this person works on. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >testing</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Person"><a href="#term_Person">Person</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Range:</th>\n <td> every value of this property is a  <span rel="rdfs:range" href="http://www.w3.org/2002/07/owl#Thing"><a href="#term_Thing">Thing</a></span>\n</td>\t</tr>\n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <!-- originally contrib\'d by Cardinal -->\n\n<p>A <code><a href=\'#term_currentProject\'>currentProject</a></code> relates a <code><a href=\'#term_Person\'>Person</a></code>\nto a <code><a href=\'#term_Document\'>Document</a></code> indicating some collaborative or\nindividual undertaking.  This relationship\nindicates that the <code><a href=\'#term_Person\'>Person</a></code> has some active role in the\nproject, such as development, coordination, or support.</p>\n\n<p>When a <code><a href=\'#term_Person\'>Person</a></code> is no longer involved with a project, or\nperhaps is inactive for some time, the relationship becomes a\n<code><a href=\'#term_pastProject\'>pastProject</a></code>.</p>\n\n<p>\nIf the <code><a href=\'#term_Person\'>Person</a></code> has stopped working on a project because it\nhas been completed (successfully or otherwise), <code><a href=\'#term_pastProject\'>pastProject</a></code> is\napplicable. In general, <code><a href=\'#term_currentProject\'>currentProject</a></code> is used to indicate\nsomeone\'s current efforts (and implied interests, concerns etc.), while\n<code><a href=\'#term_pastProject\'>pastProject</a></code> describes what they\'ve previously been doing.\n</p>\n\n<!-- \n<p>Generally speaking, anything that a <code><a href=\'#term_Person\'>Person</a></code> has\n<code><a href=\'#term_made\'>made</a></code> could also qualify as a\n<code><a href=\'#term_currentProject\'>currentProject</a></code> or <code><a href=\'#term_pastProject\'>pastProject</a></code>.</p>\n-->\n\n<p class="editorial">\nNote that this property requires further work. There has been confusion about \nwhether it points to a thing (eg. something you\'ve made; a homepage for a project, \nie. a <code><a href=\'#term_Document\'>Document</a></code> or to instances of the class <code><a href=\'#term_Project\'>Project</a></code>, \nwhich might themselves have a <code><a href=\'#term_homepage\'>homepage</a></code>. In practice, it seems to have been \nused in a similar way to <code><a href=\'#term_interest\'>interest</a></code>, referencing homepages of ongoing projects.\n</p>\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_currentProject">#</a>] <!-- currentProject currentProject --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm " id="term_depiction" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/depiction" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:depiction</h3> \n            <em>depiction</em> - A depiction of some thing. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >testing</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://www.w3.org/2002/07/owl#Thing"><a href="#term_Thing">Thing</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Range:</th>\n <td> every value of this property is a  <span rel="rdfs:range" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Image"><a href="#term_Image">Image</a></span>\n</td>\t</tr>\n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_depiction\'>depiction</a></code> property is a relationship between a thing and an \n<code><a href=\'#term_Image\'>Image</a></code> that depicts it. As such it is an inverse of the\n<code><a href=\'#term_depicts\'>depicts</a></code> relationship. \n</p>\n\n<p>\nA common use of <code><a href=\'#term_depiction\'>depiction</a></code> (and <code><a href=\'#term_depicts\'>depicts</a></code>) is to indicate \nthe contents of a digital image, for example the people or objects represented in an \nonline photo gallery. \n</p>\n\n<p>\nExtensions to this basic idea include \'<a \nhref="http://rdfweb.org/2002/01/photo/">Co-Depiction</a>\' (social networks as evidenced in \nphotos), as well as richer photo metadata through the mechanism  of using SVG paths to \nindicate the <em>regions</em> of an image which depict some particular thing. See <a \nhref="http://www.jibbering.com/svg/AnnotateImage.html">\'Annotating Images With SVG\'</a> \nfor tools and details.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nThe basic notion of \'depiction\' could also be extended to deal with multimedia content \n(video clips, audio), or refined to deal with corner cases, such as pictures of pictures etc.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_depiction\'>depiction</a></code> property is a super-property of the more specific property \n<code><a href=\'#term_img\'>img</a></code>, which is used more sparingly. You stand in a \n<code><a href=\'#term_depiction\'>depiction</a></code> relation to <em>any</em> <code><a href=\'#term_Image\'>Image</a></code> that depicts \nyou, whereas <code><a href=\'#term_img\'>img</a></code> is typically used to indicate a few images that are \nparticularly representative.\n</p>\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_depiction">#</a>] <!-- depiction depiction --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm " id="term_depicts" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/depicts" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:depicts</h3> \n            <em>depicts</em> - A thing depicted in this representation. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >testing</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Image"><a href="#term_Image">Image</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Range:</th>\n <td> every value of this property is a  <span rel="rdfs:range" href="http://www.w3.org/2002/07/owl#Thing"><a href="#term_Thing">Thing</a></span>\n</td>\t</tr>\n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_depicts\'>depicts</a></code> property is a relationship between a <code><a href=\'#term_Image\'>Image</a></code> \nand something that the image depicts. As such it is an inverse of the \n<code><a href=\'#term_depiction\'>depiction</a></code> relationship. See <code><a href=\'#term_depiction\'>depiction</a></code> for further notes.\n</p>\n\n\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_depicts">#</a>] <!-- depicts depicts --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm " id="term_familyName" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/familyName" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:familyName</h3> \n            <em>familyName</em> - The family name of some person. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >testing</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Person"><a href="#term_Person">Person</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            \n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_familyName\'>familyName</a></code> property is provided (alongside <code><a href=\'#term_givenName\'>givenName</a></code>) for use when describing parts of people\'s names. Although these\nconcepts do not capture the full range of personal naming styles found world-wide, they are commonly used and have some value.\n</p>\n<p>\nThere is also a simple <code><a href=\'#term_name\'>name</a></code> property.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nSupport is also provided for the more archaic and culturally varying terminology of <code><a href=\'#term_firstName\'>firstName</a></code> and <code><a href=\'#term_lastName\'>lastName</a></code>.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nSee the <a href="http://wiki.foaf-project.org/w/NameVocabIssue">issue tracker</a> for design discussions, status and ongoing work on rationalising the FOAF\nnaming machinery.\n</p>\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_familyName">#</a>] <!-- familyName familyName --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm " id="term_firstName" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/firstName" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:firstName</h3> \n            <em>firstName</em> - The first name of a person. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >testing</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Person"><a href="#term_Person">Person</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            \n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_firstName\'>firstName</a></code> property is provided (alongside <code><a href=\'#term_lastName\'>lastName</a></code>) as a mechanism to support legacy data that cannot be\neasily interpreted in terms of the (otherwise preferred) <code><a href=\'#term_familyName\'>familyName</a></code> and <code><a href=\'#term_givenName\'>givenName</a></code> properties. The concepts\nof \'first\' and \'last\' names do not work well across cultural and linguistic boundaries; however they are widely used in addressbooks and databases.\n</p>\n<p>\nSee the <a href="http://wiki.foaf-project.org/w/NameVocabIssue">issue tracker</a> for design discussions, status and ongoing work on rationalising the FOAF\nnaming machinery.\n</p>\n<p>\nThere is also a simple <code><a href=\'#term_name\'>name</a></code> property.\n</p>\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_firstName">#</a>] <!-- firstName firstName --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm " id="term_focus" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/focus" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:focus</h3> \n            <em>focus</em> - The underlying or \'focal\' entity associated with some SKOS-described concept. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >testing</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://www.w3.org/2004/02/skos/core#Concept"><a href="#term_Concept">Concept</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Range:</th>\n <td> every value of this property is a  <span rel="rdfs:range" href="http://www.w3.org/2002/07/owl#Thing"><a href="#term_Thing">Thing</a></span>\n</td>\t</tr>\n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_focus\'>focus</a></code> property relates a conceptualisation of something to the thing itself.\nSpecifically, it is designed for use with W3C\'s <a href="http://www.w3.org/2004/02/skos/">SKOS</a> \nvocabulary, to help indicate specific individual things (typically people, places, artifacts) \nthat are mentioned in different SKOS schemes (eg. thesauri).\n</p>\n\n<p>\nW3C SKOS is based around collections of linked \'concepts\', which indicate topics, subject areas and categories. \n<a href="http://www.w3.org/TR/2005/WD-swbp-skos-core-guide-20050510/#secmodellingrdf">In SKOS</a>,\nproperties of a skos:Concept are properties of the conceptualization (see <a href="http://www.w3.org/TR/2005/WD-swbp-skos-core-guide-20051102/#secopen">2005 discussion</a> for details); \nfor example administrative and record-keeping metadata. Two schemes might have an entry for the same individual; the foaf:focus property\ncan be used to indicate the thing in they world that they both focus on. Many SKOS concepts don\'t work this way; \nbroad topical areas and subject categories don\'t typically correspond to some particular entity. However, in cases\nwhen they do, it is useful to link both subject-oriented and thing-oriented information via foaf:focus.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nFOAF\'s focus property works alongside its other topic-oriented constructs: <code><a href=\'#term_topic\'>topic</a></code>, <code><a href=\'#term_primaryTopic\'>primaryTopic</a></code> \nare used when talking about the topical emphasis of a document. The notion of <code><a href=\'#term_primaryTopic\'>primaryTopic</a></code> \nis particularly important in FOAF as it provides an indirect mechanism for identifying things indirectly. A\nsimilar approach is explored by the <a href="http://larry.masinter.net/duri.html">TDB URI scheme</a>. FOAF includes topic-oriented \nfunctionality to address its <a href="http://www.foaf-project.org/original-intro">original goals</a> of linking people to information,\nas well as to other people, through the use of linked information.\n</p>\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_focus">#</a>] <!-- focus focus --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm " id="term_gender" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/gender" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:gender</h3> \n            <em>gender</em> - The gender of this Agent (typically but not necessarily \'male\' or \'female\'). <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >testing</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Agent"><a href="#term_Agent">Agent</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            \n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr><tr><th colspan="2">Functional Property</th>\n <td> <span rel="rdf:type" href="http://www.w3.org/2002/07/owl#FunctionalProperty"></span> </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_gender\'>gender</a></code> property relates a <code><a href=\'#term_Agent\'>Agent</a></code> (typically a \n<code><a href=\'#term_Person\'>Person</a></code>) to a string representing its gender. In most cases the value \nwill be the string \'female\' or \'male\' (in lowercase without surrounding quotes or spaces). \nLike all FOAF properties, there is in general no requirement to use  \n<code><a href=\'#term_gender\'>gender</a></code> in any particular document or description. Values other than \n\'male\' and \'female\' may be used, but are not enumerated here. The <code><a href=\'#term_gender\'>gender</a></code> \nmechanism is not intended to capture the full variety of biological, social and sexual \nconcepts associated with the word \'gender\'. \n</p>\n\n<p>\nAnything that has a <code><a href=\'#term_gender\'>gender</a></code> property will be some kind of \n<code><a href=\'#term_Agent\'>Agent</a></code>. However there are kinds of <code><a href=\'#term_Agent\'>Agent</a></code> to \nwhich the concept of gender isn\'t applicable (eg. a <code><a href=\'#term_Group\'>Group</a></code>). FOAF does not \ncurrently include a class corresponding directly to "the type of thing that has a gender".\nAt any point in time, a <code><a href=\'#term_Agent\'>Agent</a></code> has at most one value for \n<code><a href=\'#term_gender\'>gender</a></code>. FOAF does not treat <code><a href=\'#term_gender\'>gender</a></code> as a \n<em>static</em> property; the same individual may have different values for this property \nat different times.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nNote that FOAF\'s notion of gender isn\'t defined biologically or anatomically - this would \nbe tricky since we have a broad notion that applies to all <code><a href=\'#term_Agent\'>Agent</a></code>s \n(including robots - eg. Bender from Futurama is \'male\'). As stressed above, \nFOAF\'s notion of gender doesn\'t attempt to encompass the full range of concepts associated \nwith human gender, biology and sexuality. As such it is a (perhaps awkward) compromise \nbetween the clinical and the social/psychological. In general, a person will be the best \nauthority on their <code><a href=\'#term_gender\'>gender</a></code>. Feedback on this design is \nparticularly welcome (via the FOAF mailing list, \n<a href="http://lists.foaf-project.org/mailman/listinfo/foaf-dev">foaf-dev</a>). We have tried to \nbe respectful of diversity without attempting to catalogue or enumerate that diversity. \n</p>\n\n<p>\nThis may also be a good point for a periodic reminder: as with all FOAF properties, \ndocuments that use \'<code><a href=\'#term_gender\'>gender</a></code>\' will on occassion be innacurate, misleading \nor outright false. FOAF, like all open means of communication, supports <em>lying</em>.\n Application authors using \nFOAF data should always be cautious in their presentation of unverified information, but be \nparticularly sensitive to issues and risks surrounding sex and gender (including privacy \nand personal safety concerns). Designers of FOAF-based user interfaces should be careful to allow users to omit \n<code><a href=\'#term_gender\'>gender</a></code> when describing themselves and others, and to allow at least for \nvalues other than \'male\' and \'female\' as options. Users of information \nconveyed via FOAF (as via information conveyed through mobile phone text messages, email, \nInternet chat, HTML pages etc.) should be skeptical of unverified information.\n</p>\n \n<!--\nb/g article currently offline.\nhttp://216.239.37.104/search?q=cache:Q_P_fH8M0swJ:archives.thedaily.washington.edu/1997/042997/sex.042997.html+%22Lois+McDermott%22&hl=en&start=7&ie=UTF-8\n-->\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_gender">#</a>] <!-- gender gender --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm " id="term_givenName" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/givenName" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:givenName</h3> \n            <em>Given name</em> - The given name of some person. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >testing</span></td></tr>\n            \n            \n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_givenName\'>givenName</a></code> property is provided (alongside <code><a href=\'#term_familyName\'>familyName</a></code>) for use when describing parts of people\'s names. Although these\nconcepts do not capture the full range of personal naming styles found world-wide, they are commonly used and have some value. \n</p>\n<p>\nThere is also a simple <code><a href=\'#term_name\'>name</a></code> property.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nSupport is also provided for the more archaic and culturally varying terminology of <code><a href=\'#term_firstName\'>firstName</a></code> and <code><a href=\'#term_lastName\'>lastName</a></code>.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nSee the <a href="http://wiki.foaf-project.org/w/NameVocabIssue">issue tracker</a> for design discussions, status and ongoing work on rationalising the FOAF\nnaming machinery.\n</p>\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_givenName">#</a>] <!-- givenName givenName --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm " id="term_icqChatID" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/icqChatID" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:icqChatID</h3> \n            <em>ICQ chat ID</em> - An ICQ chat ID <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >testing</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Agent"><a href="#term_Agent">Agent</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            \n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr><tr><th colspan="2">Inverse Functional Property</th>\n <td> <span rel="rdf:type" href="http://www.w3.org/2002/07/owl#InverseFunctionalProperty"></span> </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_icqChatID\'>icqChatID</a></code> property relates a <code><a href=\'#term_Agent\'>Agent</a></code> to a textual \nidentifier assigned to them in the ICQ Chat system. \nSee the <a href="http://web.icq.com/icqchat/">icq chat</a> site for more details of the \'icq\' \nservice. Their "<a href="http://www.icq.com/products/whatisicq.html">What is ICQ?</a>" document \nprovides a basic overview, while their "<a href="http://company.icq.com/info/">About Us</a> page \nnotes that ICQ has been acquired by AOL. Despite the relationship with AOL, ICQ is at \nthe time of writing maintained as a separate identity from the AIM brand (see \n<code><a href=\'#term_aimChatID\'>aimChatID</a></code>).\n</p>\n\n<p>\nSee <code><a href=\'#term_OnlineChatAccount\'>OnlineChatAccount</a></code> (and <code><a href=\'#term_OnlineAccount\'>OnlineAccount</a></code>) for a\nmore general (and verbose) mechanism for describing IM and chat accounts.\n</p>\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_icqChatID">#</a>] <!-- icqChatID icqChatID --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm " id="term_img" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/img" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:img</h3> \n            <em>image</em> - An image that can be used to represent some thing (ie. those depictions which are particularly representative of something, eg. one\'s photo on a homepage). <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >testing</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Person"><a href="#term_Person">Person</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Range:</th>\n <td> every value of this property is a  <span rel="rdfs:range" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Image"><a href="#term_Image">Image</a></span>\n</td>\t</tr>\n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_img\'>img</a></code> property relates a <code><a href=\'#term_Person\'>Person</a></code> to a \n<code><a href=\'#term_Image\'>Image</a></code> that represents them. Unlike its super-property \n<code><a href=\'#term_depiction\'>depiction</a></code>, we only use <code><a href=\'#term_img\'>img</a></code> when an image is \nparticularly representative of some person. The analogy is with the image(s) that might \nappear on someone\'s homepage, rather than happen to appear somewhere in their photo album.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nUnlike the more general <code><a href=\'#term_depiction\'>depiction</a></code> property (and its inverse, \n<code><a href=\'#term_depicts\'>depicts</a></code>), the <code><a href=\'#term_img\'>img</a></code> property is only used with \nrepresentations of people (ie. instances of <code><a href=\'#term_Person\'>Person</a></code>). So you can\'t use \nit to find pictures of cats, dogs etc. The basic idea is to have a term whose use is more \nrestricted than <code><a href=\'#term_depiction\'>depiction</a></code> so we can have a useful way of picking out a \nreasonable image to represent someone. FOAF defines <code><a href=\'#term_img\'>img</a></code> as a \nsub-property of <code><a href=\'#term_depiction\'>depiction</a></code>, which means that the latter relationship is \nimplied whenever two things are related by the former.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nNote that <code><a href=\'#term_img\'>img</a></code> does not have any restrictions on the dimensions, colour \ndepth, format etc of the <code><a href=\'#term_Image\'>Image</a></code> it references. \n</p>\n\n<p>\nTerminology: note that <code><a href=\'#term_img\'>img</a></code> is a property (ie. relationship), and that \n<code>code:Image</code> is a similarly named class (ie. category, a type of thing). It \nmight have been more helpful to call <code><a href=\'#term_img\'>img</a></code> \'mugshot\' or similar; instead \nit is named by analogy to the HTML IMG element.\n</p> \n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_img">#</a>] <!-- img img --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm " id="term_interest" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/interest" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:interest</h3> \n            <em>interest</em> - A page about a topic of interest to this person. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >testing</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Agent"><a href="#term_Agent">Agent</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Range:</th>\n <td> every value of this property is a  <span rel="rdfs:range" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Document"><a href="#term_Document">Document</a></span>\n</td>\t</tr>\n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_interest\'>interest</a></code> property represents an interest of a \n<code><a href=\'#term_Agent\'>Agent</a></code>, through \nindicating a <code><a href=\'#term_Document\'>Document</a></code> whose <code><a href=\'#term_topic\'>topic</a></code>(s) broadly \ncharacterises that interest.</p>\n\n<p class="example">\nFor example, we might claim that a person or group has an interest in RDF by saying they\nstand in a <code><a href=\'#term_interest\'>interest</a></code> relationship to the <a \nhref="http://www.w3.org/RDF/">RDF</a> home page. Loosly, such RDF would be saying \n<em>"this agent is interested in the topic of this page"</em>.\n</p>\n\n<p class="example">\nUses of <code><a href=\'#term_interest\'>interest</a></code> include a variety of filtering and resource discovery \napplications. It could be used, for example, to help find answers to questions such as \n"Find me members of this organisation with an interest in XML who have also contributed to \n<a href="http://www.cpan.org/">CPAN</a>)". \n</p>\n\n<p>\nThis approach to characterising interests is intended to compliment other mechanisms (such \nas the use of controlled vocabulary). It allows us to use a widely known set of unique \nidentifiers (Web page URIs) with minimal pre-coordination. Since URIs have a controlled \nsyntax, this makes data merging much easier than the use of free-text characterisations of \ninterest.\n</p>\n\n\n<p>\nNote that interest does not imply expertise, and that this FOAF term provides no support \nfor characterising levels of interest: passing fads and lifelong quests are both examples \nof someone\'s <code><a href=\'#term_interest\'>interest</a></code>. Describing interests in full is a complex \nundertaking; <code><a href=\'#term_interest\'>interest</a></code> provides one basic component of FOAF\'s approach to \nthese problems.\n</p>\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_interest">#</a>] <!-- interest interest --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm " id="term_jabberID" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/jabberID" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:jabberID</h3> \n            <em>jabber ID</em> - A jabber ID for something. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >testing</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Agent"><a href="#term_Agent">Agent</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            \n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr><tr><th colspan="2">Inverse Functional Property</th>\n <td> <span rel="rdf:type" href="http://www.w3.org/2002/07/owl#InverseFunctionalProperty"></span> </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_jabberID\'>jabberID</a></code> property relates a <code><a href=\'#term_Agent\'>Agent</a></code> to a textual \nidentifier assigned to them in the <a href="http://www.jabber.org/">Jabber</a> messaging \nsystem. \nSee the <a href="http://www.jabber.org/">Jabber</a> site for more information about the Jabber \nprotocols and tools.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nJabber, unlike several other online messaging systems, is based on an open, publically \ndocumented protocol specification, and has a variety of open source implementations. Jabber IDs \ncan be assigned to a variety of kinds of thing, including software \'bots\', chat rooms etc. For \nthe purposes of FOAF, these are all considered to be kinds of <code><a href=\'#term_Agent\'>Agent</a></code> (ie. \nthings that <em>do</em> stuff). The uses of Jabber go beyond simple IM chat applications. The \n<code><a href=\'#term_jabberID\'>jabberID</a></code> property is provided as a basic hook to help support RDF description \nof Jabber users and services.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nSee <code><a href=\'#term_OnlineChatAccount\'>OnlineChatAccount</a></code> (and <code><a href=\'#term_OnlineAccount\'>OnlineAccount</a></code>) for a\nmore general (and verbose) mechanism for describing IM and chat accounts.\n</p>\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_jabberID">#</a>] <!-- jabberID jabberID --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm " id="term_lastName" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/lastName" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:lastName</h3> \n            <em>lastName</em> - The last name of a person. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >testing</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Person"><a href="#term_Person">Person</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            \n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_lastName\'>lastName</a></code> property is provided (alongside <code><a href=\'#term_firstName\'>firstName</a></code>) as a mechanism to support legacy data that cannot be\neasily interpreted in terms of the (otherwise preferred) <code><a href=\'#term_familyName\'>familyName</a></code> and <code><a href=\'#term_givenName\'>givenName</a></code> properties. The concepts \nof \'first\' and \'last\' names do not work well across cultural and linguistic boundaries; however they are widely used in addressbooks and databases.\n</p>\n<p>\nSee the <a href="http://wiki.foaf-project.org/w/NameVocabIssue">issue tracker</a> for design discussions, status and ongoing work on rationalising the FOAF \nnaming machinery.\n</p>\n<p>\nThere is also a simple <code><a href=\'#term_name\'>name</a></code> property.\n</p>\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_logo">#</a>] <!-- logo logo --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm " id="term_mbox_sha1sum" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/mbox_sha1sum" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:mbox_sha1sum</h3> \n            <em>sha1sum of a personal mailbox URI name</em> - The sha1sum of the URI of an Internet mailbox associated with exactly one owner, the  first owner of the mailbox. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >testing</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Agent"><a href="#term_Agent">Agent</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            \n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr><tr><th colspan="2">Inverse Functional Property</th>\n <td> <span rel="rdf:type" href="http://www.w3.org/2002/07/owl#InverseFunctionalProperty"></span> </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nA <code><a href=\'#term_mbox_sha1sum\'>mbox_sha1sum</a></code> of a <code><a href=\'#term_Person\'>Person</a></code> is a textual representation of \nthe result of applying the SHA1 mathematical functional to a \'mailto:\' identifier (URI) for an \nInternet mailbox that they stand in a <code><a href=\'#term_mbox\'>mbox</a></code> relationship to.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nIn other words, if you have a mailbox (<code><a href=\'#term_mbox\'>mbox</a></code>) but don\'t want to reveal its \naddress, you can take that address and generate a <code><a href=\'#term_mbox_sha1sum\'>mbox_sha1sum</a></code> representation \nof it. Just as a <code><a href=\'#term_mbox\'>mbox</a></code> can be used as an indirect identifier for its owner, we \ncan do the same with <code><a href=\'#term_mbox_sha1sum\'>mbox_sha1sum</a></code> since there is only one \n<code><a href=\'#term_Person\'>Person</a></code> with any particular value for that property.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nMany FOAF tools use <code><a href=\'#term_mbox_sha1sum\'>mbox_sha1sum</a></code> in preference to exposing mailbox \ninformation. This is usually for privacy and SPAM-avoidance reasons. Other relevant techniques \ninclude the use of PGP encryption (see <a href="http://usefulinc.com/foaf/">Edd Dumbill\'s \ndocumentation</a>) and the use of <a \nhref="http://www.w3.org/2001/12/rubyrdf/util/foafwhite/intro.html">FOAF-based whitelists</a> for \nmail filtering.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nCode examples for SHA1 in C#, Java, PHP, Perl and Python can be found <a \nhref="http://www.intertwingly.net/blog/1545.html">in Sam Ruby\'s \nweblog entry</a>. Remember to include the \'mailto:\' prefix, but no trailing \nwhitespace, when computing a <code><a href=\'#term_mbox_sha1sum\'>mbox_sha1sum</a></code> property.\n</p>\n<!-- what about Javascript. move refs to wiki maybe. -->\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_mbox_sha1sum">#</a>] <!-- mbox_sha1sum mbox_sha1sum --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm " id="term_msnChatID" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/msnChatID" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:msnChatID</h3> \n            <em>MSN chat ID</em> - An MSN chat ID <br /><table style="th { float: top; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_msnChatID">#</a>] <!-- msnChatID msnChatID --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm " id="term_myersBriggs" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/myersBriggs" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:myersBriggs</h3> \n            <em>myersBriggs</em> - A Myers Briggs (MBTI) personality classification. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >testing</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Person"><a href="#term_Person">Person</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            \n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <!-- todo: expand acronym -->\n\n<p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_myersBriggs\'>myersBriggs</a></code> property represents the Myers Briggs (MBTI) approach to \npersonality taxonomy. It is included in FOAF as an example of a property \nthat takes certain constrained values, and to give some additional detail to the FOAF \nfiles of those who choose to include it. The <code><a href=\'#term_myersBriggs\'>myersBriggs</a></code> property applies only to the \n<code><a href=\'#term_Person\'>Person</a></code> class; wherever you see it, you can infer it is being applied to \na person.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_myersBriggs\'>myersBriggs</a></code> property is interesting in that it illustrates how \nFOAF can serve as a carrier for various kinds of information, without necessarily being \ncommited to any associated worldview. Not everyone will find myersBriggs (or star signs, \nor blood types, or the four humours) a useful perspective on human behaviour and \npersonality. The inclusion of a Myers Briggs property doesn\'t indicate that FOAF endorses \nthe underlying theory, any more than the existence of <code><a href=\'#term_weblog\'>weblog</a></code> is an \nendorsement of soapboxes.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nThe values for <code><a href=\'#term_myersBriggs\'>myersBriggs</a></code> are the following 16 4-letter textual codes: \nESTJ, INFP, ESFP, INTJ, ESFJ, INTP, ENFP, ISTJ, ESTP, INFJ, ENFJ, ISTP, ENTJ, ISFP, \nENTP, ISFJ. If multiple of these properties are applicable, they are represented by \napplying multiple properties to a person.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nFor further reading on MBTI, see various online sources (eg. <a \nhref="http://www.teamtechnology.co.uk/tt/t-articl/mb-simpl.htm">this article</a>). There \nare various online sites which offer quiz-based tools for determining a person\'s MBTI \nclassification. The owners of the MBTI trademark have probably not approved of these.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nThis FOAF property suggests some interesting uses, some of which could perhaps be used to \ntest the claims made by proponents of the MBTI (eg. an analysis of weblog postings \nfiltered by MBTI type). However it should be noted that MBTI FOAF descriptions are \nself-selecting; MBTI categories may not be uniformly appealing to the people they \ndescribe. Further, there is probably a degree of cultural specificity implicit in the \nassumptions made by many questionaire-based MBTI tools; the MBTI system may not make \nsense in cultural settings beyond those it was created for. \n</p>\n\n<p>\nSee also <a href="http://webspace.webring.com/people/cl/lifexplore/mbintro.htm">Cory Caplinger\'s summary table</a> or the RDFWeb article, <a \nhref="http://web.archive.org/web/20080802184922/http://rdfweb.org/mt/foaflog/archives/000004.html">FOAF Myers Briggs addition</a> \nfor further background and examples.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nNote: Myers Briggs Type Indicator and MBTI are registered trademarks of Consulting \nPsychologists Press Inc. Oxford Psycholgists Press Ltd has exclusive rights to the \ntrademark in the UK. \n</p>\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_myersBriggs">#</a>] <!-- myersBriggs myersBriggs --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm " id="term_name" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/name" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:name</h3> \n            <em>name</em> - A name for some thing. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >testing</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://www.w3.org/2002/07/owl#Thing"><a href="#term_Thing">Thing</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            \n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>The <code><a href=\'#term_name\'>name</a></code> of something is a simple textual string.</p>\n\n<p>\nXML language tagging may be used to indicate the language of the name. For example:\n</p>\n\n<div class="example">\n<code>\n&lt;foaf:name xml:lang="en"&gt;Dan Brickley&lt;/foaf:name&gt;\n</code>\n</div>\n\n<p>\nFOAF provides some other naming constructs.  While foaf:name does not explicitly represent name substructure (family vs given etc.) it \ndoes provide a basic level of interoperability. See the <a \nhref="http://wiki.foaf-project.org/w/IssueTracker">issue tracker</a> for status of work on this issue.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_name\'>name</a></code> property, like all RDF properties with a range of rdfs:Literal, may be used with XMLLiteral datatyped \nvalues (multiple <code><a href=\'#term_name\'>name</a></code>s are acceptable whether they are in the same langauge or not). XMLLiteral usage is not yet widely adopted. Feedback on this aspect of the FOAF design is particularly welcomed.\n</p>\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_name">#</a>] <!-- name name --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm " id="term_nick" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/nick" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:nick</h3> \n            <em>nickname</em> - A short informal nickname characterising an agent (includes login identifiers, IRC and other chat nicknames). <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >testing</span></td></tr>\n            \n            \n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_nick\'>nick</a></code> property relates a <code><a href=\'#term_Person\'>Person</a></code> to a short (often \nabbreviated) nickname, such as those use in IRC chat, online accounts, and computer \nlogins.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nThis property is necessarily vague, because it does not indicate any particular naming \ncontrol authority, and so cannot distinguish a person\'s login from their (possibly \nvarious) IRC nicknames or other similar identifiers. However it has some utility, since \nmany people use the same string (or slight variants) across a variety of such \nenvironments.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nFor specific controlled sets of names (relating primarily to Instant Messanger accounts), \nFOAF provides some convenience properties: <code><a href=\'#term_jabberID\'>jabberID</a></code>, \n<code><a href=\'#term_aimChatID\'>aimChatID</a></code>, <code><a href=\'#term_msnChatID\'>msnChatID</a></code> and \n<code><a href=\'#term_icqChatID\'>icqChatID</a></code>. Beyond this, the problem of representing such accounts is not \npeculiar to Instant Messanging, and it is not scaleable to attempt to enumerate each \nnaming database as a distinct FOAF property. The <code><a href=\'#term_OnlineAccount\'>OnlineAccount</a></code> term (and \nsupporting vocabulary) are provided as a more verbose and more expressive generalisation \nof these properties.\n</p>\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_nick">#</a>] <!-- nick nick --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm " id="term_openid" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/openid" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:openid</h3> \n            <em>openid</em> - An OpenID for an Agent. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >testing</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Agent"><a href="#term_Agent">Agent</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Range:</th>\n <td> every value of this property is a  <span rel="rdfs:range" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Document"><a href="#term_Document">Document</a></span>\n</td>\t</tr>\n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr><tr><th colspan="2">Inverse Functional Property</th>\n <td> <span rel="rdf:type" href="http://www.w3.org/2002/07/owl#InverseFunctionalProperty"></span> </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nA <code><a href=\'#term_openid\'>openid</a></code> is a property of a <code><a href=\'#term_Agent\'>Agent</a></code> that associates it with a document that can be used as an <a \nhref="http://www.w3.org/TR/webarch/#indirect-identification">indirect identifier</a> in the manner of the <a href="http://openid.net/specs/openid-authentication-1_1.html">OpenID</a>\n "Identity URL". As the OpenID 1.1 specification notes, OpenID itself"<em>does not provide any mechanism to \nexchange profile information, though Consumers of an Identity can learn more about an End User from any public, semantically \ninteresting documents linked thereunder (FOAF, RSS, Atom, vCARD, etc.)</em>". In this way, FOAF and OpenID complement each other; \nneither provides a stand-alone approach to online "trust", but combined they can address interesting parts of this larger problem \nspace.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_openid\'>openid</a></code> property is "inverse functional", meaning that anything that is the foaf:openid of something, is the \n<code><a href=\'#term_openid\'>openid</a></code> of no more than one thing. FOAF is agnostic as to whether there are (according to the relevant OpenID \nspecifications) OpenID URIs that are equally associated with multiple Agents. FOAF offers sub-classes of Agent, ie. \n<code><a href=\'#term_Organization\'>Organization</a></code> and <code><a href=\'#term_Group\'>Group</a></code>, that allow for such scenarios to be consistent with the notion that any \nfoaf:openid is the foaf:openid of just one <code><a href=\'#term_Agent\'>Agent</a></code>.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nFOAF does not mandate any particular URI scheme for use as <code><a href=\'#term_openid\'>openid</a></code> values. The OpenID 1.1 specification includes a <a \nhref="http://openid.net/specs/openid-authentication-1_1.html#delegating_authentication">delegation model</a> that is often used to \nallow a weblog or homepage document to also serve in OpenID authentication via "link rel" HTML markup. This deployment model provides a \nconvenient connection to FOAF, since a similar <a href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/spec/#sec-autodesc">technique</a> is used for FOAF \nautodiscovery in HTML. A single document can, for example, serve both as a homepage and an OpenID identity URL.\n</p>\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_openid">#</a>] <!-- openid openid --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm " id="term_pastProject" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/pastProject" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:pastProject</h3> \n            <em>past project</em> - A project this person has previously worked on. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >testing</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Person"><a href="#term_Person">Person</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Range:</th>\n <td> every value of this property is a  <span rel="rdfs:range" href="http://www.w3.org/2002/07/owl#Thing"><a href="#term_Thing">Thing</a></span>\n</td>\t</tr>\n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>After a <code><a href=\'#term_Person\'>Person</a></code> is no longer involved with a\n<code><a href=\'#term_currentProject\'>currentProject</a></code>, or has been inactive for some time, a\n<code><a href=\'#term_pastProject\'>pastProject</a></code> relationship can be used. This indicates that\nthe <code><a href=\'#term_Person\'>Person</a></code> was involved with the described project at one\npoint. \n</p>\n\n<p>\nIf the <code><a href=\'#term_Person\'>Person</a></code> has stopped working on a project because it \nhas been completed (successfully or otherwise), <code><a href=\'#term_pastProject\'>pastProject</a></code> is \napplicable. In general, <code><a href=\'#term_currentProject\'>currentProject</a></code> is used to indicate \nsomeone\'s current efforts (and implied interests, concerns etc.), while \n<code><a href=\'#term_pastProject\'>pastProject</a></code> describes what they\'ve previously been doing.\n</p>\n\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_pastProject">#</a>] <!-- pastProject pastProject --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm " id="term_phone" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/phone" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:phone</h3> \n            <em>phone</em> - A phone,  specified using fully qualified tel: URI scheme (refs: http://www.w3.org/Addressing/schemes.html#tel). <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >testing</span></td></tr>\n            \n            \n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_phone\'>phone</a></code> of something is a phone, typically identified using the tel: URI \nscheme.\n</p>\n\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_phone">#</a>] <!-- phone phone --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm " id="term_plan" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/plan" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:plan</h3> \n            <em>plan</em> - A .plan comment, in the tradition of finger and \'.plan\' files. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >testing</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Person"><a href="#term_Person">Person</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            \n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <!-- orig contrib\'d by Cardinal -->\n\n<p>The <code><a href=\'#term_plan\'>plan</a></code> property provides a space for a\n<code><a href=\'#term_Person\'>Person</a></code> to hold some arbitrary content that would appear in\na traditional \'.plan\' file.  The plan file was stored in a user\'s home\ndirectory on a UNIX machine, and displayed to people when the user was\nqueried with the finger utility.</p>\n\n<p>A plan file could contain anything.  Typical uses included brief\ncomments, thoughts, or remarks on what a person had been doing lately.  Plan\nfiles were also prone to being witty or simply osbscure.  Others may be more\ncreative, writing any number of seemingly random compositions in their plan\nfile for people to stumble upon.</p>\n\n<p>\nSee <a href="http://www.rajivshah.com/Case_Studies/Finger/Finger.htm">History of the \nFinger Protocol</a> by Rajiv Shah for more on this piece of Internet history. The \n<code><a href=\'#term_geekcode\'>geekcode</a></code> property may also be of interest.\n</p>\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_plan">#</a>] <!-- plan plan --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm " id="term_publications" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/publications" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:publications</h3> \n            <em>publications</em> - A link to the publications of this person. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >testing</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Person"><a href="#term_Person">Person</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Range:</th>\n <td> every value of this property is a  <span rel="rdfs:range" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Document"><a href="#term_Document">Document</a></span>\n</td>\t</tr>\n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_publications\'>publications</a></code> property indicates a <code><a href=\'#term_Document\'>Document</a></code>\nlisting (primarily in human-readable form) some publications associated with the  \n<code><a href=\'#term_Person\'>Person</a></code>. Such documents are typically published alongside one\'s \n<code><a href=\'#term_homepage\'>homepage</a></code>.\n</p>\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_publications">#</a>] <!-- publications publications --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm " id="term_schoolHomepage" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/schoolHomepage" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:schoolHomepage</h3> \n            <em>schoolHomepage</em> - A homepage of a school attended by the person. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >testing</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Person"><a href="#term_Person">Person</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Range:</th>\n <td> every value of this property is a  <span rel="rdfs:range" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Document"><a href="#term_Document">Document</a></span>\n</td>\t</tr>\n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe <code>schoolHomepage</code> property relates a <code><a href=\'#term_Person\'>Person</a></code> to a \n<code><a href=\'#term_Document\'>Document</a></code> that is the <code><a href=\'#term_homepage\'>homepage</a></code> of a School that the \nperson attended.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nFOAF does not (currently) define a class for \'School\' (if it did, it would probably be as \na sub-class of <code><a href=\'#term_Organization\'>Organization</a></code>). The original application area for \n<code><a href=\'#term_schoolHomepage\'>schoolHomepage</a></code> was for \'schools\' in the British-English sense; however \nAmerican-English usage has dominated, and it is now perfectly reasonable to describe \nUniversities, Colleges and post-graduate study using <code><a href=\'#term_schoolHomepage\'>schoolHomepage</a></code>.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nThis very basic facility provides a basis for a low-cost, decentralised approach to \nclassmate-reunion and suchlike. Instead of requiring a central database, we can use FOAF \nto express claims such as \'I studied <em>here</em>\' simply by mentioning a \nschool\'s homepage within FOAF files. Given the homepage of a school, it is easy for \nFOAF aggregators to lookup this property in search of people who attended that school.\n</p>\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_schoolHomepage">#</a>] <!-- schoolHomepage schoolHomepage --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm " id="term_skypeID" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/skypeID" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:skypeID</h3> \n            <em>Skype ID</em> - A Skype ID <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >testing</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Agent"><a href="#term_Agent">Agent</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            \n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_skype\'>skype</a></code> property relates a <code><a href=\'#term_Agent\'>Agent</a></code> to an account name of \na Skype account of theirs.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nSee <code><a href=\'#term_OnlineChatAccount\'>OnlineChatAccount</a></code> (and <code><a href=\'#term_OnlineAccount\'>OnlineAccount</a></code>) for a\nmore general (and verbose) mechanism for describing IM and chat accounts.\n</p>\n\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_skypeID">#</a>] <!-- skypeID skypeID --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm " id="term_thumbnail" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/thumbnail" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:thumbnail</h3> \n            <em>thumbnail</em> - A derived thumbnail image. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >testing</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Image"><a href="#term_Image">Image</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Range:</th>\n <td> every value of this property is a  <span rel="rdfs:range" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Image"><a href="#term_Image">Image</a></span>\n</td>\t</tr>\n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <!-- originally contrib\'d by Cardinal; rejiggged a bit by danbri -->\n\n<p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_thumbnail\'>thumbnail</a></code> property is a relationship between a \nfull-size <code><a href=\'#term_Image\'>Image</a></code> and a smaller, representative <code><a href=\'#term_Image\'>Image</a></code> \nthat has been derrived from it. \n</p>\n\n<p>\nIt is typical in FOAF to express <code><a href=\'#term_img\'>img</a></code> and <code><a href=\'#term_depiction\'>depiction</a></code> \nrelationships in terms of the larger, \'main\' (in some sense) image, rather than its thumbnail(s). \nA <code><a href=\'#term_thumbnail\'>thumbnail</a></code> might be clipped or otherwise reduced such that it does not \ndepict everything that the full image depicts. Therefore FOAF does not specify that a \nthumbnail <code><a href=\'#term_depicts\'>depicts</a></code> everything that the image it is derrived from depicts.\nHowever, FOAF does expect that anything depicted in the thumbnail will also be depicted in \nthe source image.\n</p>\n\n<!-- todo: add RDF rules here showing this -->\n\n<p>\nA <code><a href=\'#term_thumbnail\'>thumbnail</a></code> is typically small enough that it can be\nloaded and viewed quickly before a viewer decides to download the larger\nversion. They are often used in online photo gallery applications.\n</p>\n\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_thumbnail">#</a>] <!-- thumbnail thumbnail --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm " id="term_tipjar" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/tipjar" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:tipjar</h3> \n            <em>tipjar</em> - A tipjar document for this agent, describing means for payment and reward. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >testing</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Agent"><a href="#term_Agent">Agent</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Range:</th>\n <td> every value of this property is a  <span rel="rdfs:range" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Document"><a href="#term_Document">Document</a></span>\n</td>\t</tr>\n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_tipjar\'>tipjar</a></code> property relates an <code><a href=\'#term_Agent\'>Agent</a></code>\nto a <code><a href=\'#term_Document\'>Document</a></code> that describes some mechanisms for\npaying or otherwise rewarding that agent.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_tipjar\'>tipjar</a></code> property was created following <a\nhref="http://rdfweb.org/mt/foaflog/archives/2004/02/12/20.07.32/">discussions</a> \nabout simple, lightweight mechanisms that could be used to encourage \nrewards and payment for content exchanged online. An agent\'s\n<code><a href=\'#term_tipjar\'>tipjar</a></code> page(s) could describe informal ("Send me a\npostcard!", "here\'s my book, music and movie wishlist") or formal\n(machine-readable micropayment information) information about how that\nagent can be paid or rewarded. The reward is not associated with any\nparticular action or content from the agent concerned. A link to \na service such as <a href="http://www.paypal.com/">PayPal</a> is the\nsort of thing we might expect to find in a tipjar document.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nNote that the value of a <code><a href=\'#term_tipjar\'>tipjar</a></code> property is just a\ndocument (which can include anchors into HTML pages). We expect, but \ndo not currently specify, that this will evolve into a hook\nfor finding more machine-readable information to support payments,\nrewards. The <code><a href=\'#term_OnlineAccount\'>OnlineAccount</a></code> machinery is also relevant,\nalthough the information requirements for automating payments are not \ncurrently clear.\n</p>\n\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_tipjar">#</a>] <!-- tipjar tipjar --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm " id="term_title" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/title" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:title</h3> \n            <em>title</em> - Title (Mr, Mrs, Ms, Dr. etc) <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >testing</span></td></tr>\n            \n            \n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p class="editorial">\nThis property is a candidate for deprecation in favour of \'honorificPrefix\' following Portable Contacts usage. See the <a href="http://wiki.foaf-project.org/w/IssueTracker">FOAF Issue Tracker</a>.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nThe approriate values for <code><a href=\'#term_title\'>title</a></code> are not formally constrained, and will \nvary across community and context. Values such as \'Mr\', \'Mrs\', \'Ms\', \'Dr\' etc. are \nexpected.\n</p>\n\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_title">#</a>] <!-- title title --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm " id="term_topic" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/topic" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:topic</h3> \n            <em>topic</em> - A topic of some page or document. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >testing</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Document"><a href="#term_Document">Document</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Range:</th>\n <td> every value of this property is a  <span rel="rdfs:range" href="http://www.w3.org/2002/07/owl#Thing"><a href="#term_Thing">Thing</a></span>\n</td>\t</tr>\n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_topic\'>topic</a></code> property relates a document to a thing that the document is \nabout.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nAs such it is an inverse of the <code><a href=\'#term_page\'>page</a></code> property, which relates a thing to \na document about that thing.\n</p>\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_topic">#</a>] <!-- topic topic --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm " id="term_topic_interest" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/topic_interest" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:topic_interest</h3> \n            <em>topic_interest</em> - A thing of interest to this person. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >testing</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Agent"><a href="#term_Agent">Agent</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Range:</th>\n <td> every value of this property is a  <span rel="rdfs:range" href="http://www.w3.org/2002/07/owl#Thing"><a href="#term_Thing">Thing</a></span>\n</td>\t</tr>\n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_topic_interest\'>topic_interest</a></code> property links a <code><a href=\'#term_Agent\'>Agent</a></code> to a thing that they\'re interested in. \nUnlike <code><a href=\'#term_topic\'>topic</a></code> it is not indirected through a document, but links the thing directly.\n</p>\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_topic_interest">#</a>] <!-- topic_interest topic_interest --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm " id="term_workInfoHomepage" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/workInfoHomepage" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:workInfoHomepage</h3> \n            <em>work info homepage</em> - A work info homepage of some person; a page about their work for some organization. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >testing</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Person"><a href="#term_Person">Person</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Range:</th>\n <td> every value of this property is a  <span rel="rdfs:range" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Document"><a href="#term_Document">Document</a></span>\n</td>\t</tr>\n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_workInfoHomepage\'>workInfoHomepage</a></code> of a <code><a href=\'#term_Person\'>Person</a></code> is a \n<code><a href=\'#term_Document\'>Document</a></code> that describes their work. It is generally (but not necessarily) a \ndifferent document from their <code><a href=\'#term_homepage\'>homepage</a></code>, and from any \n<code><a href=\'#term_workplaceHomepage\'>workplaceHomepage</a></code>(s) they may have. \n</p>\n\n<p>\nThe purpose of this property is to distinguish those pages you often see, which describe \nsomeone\'s professional role within an organisation or project. These aren\'t really homepages, \nalthough they share some characterstics.\n</p>\n\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_workInfoHomepage">#</a>] <!-- workInfoHomepage workInfoHomepage --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm " id="term_workplaceHomepage" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/workplaceHomepage" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:workplaceHomepage</h3> \n            <em>workplace homepage</em> - A workplace homepage of some person; the homepage of an organization they work for. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >testing</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Person"><a href="#term_Person">Person</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Range:</th>\n <td> every value of this property is a  <span rel="rdfs:range" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Document"><a href="#term_Document">Document</a></span>\n</td>\t</tr>\n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_workplaceHomepage\'>workplaceHomepage</a></code> of a <code><a href=\'#term_Person\'>Person</a></code> is a \n<code><a href=\'#term_Document\'>Document</a></code> that is the <code><a href=\'#term_homepage\'>homepage</a></code> of a \n<code><a href=\'#term_Organization\'>Organization</a></code> that they work for.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nBy directly relating people to the homepages of their workplace, we have a simple convention \nthat takes advantage of a set of widely known identifiers, while taking care not to confuse the \nthings those identifiers identify (ie. organizational homepages) with the actual organizations \nthose homepages describe.\n</p>\n\n<div class="example">\n<p>\nFor example, Dan Brickley works at W3C. Dan is a <code><a href=\'#term_Person\'>Person</a></code> with a \n<code><a href=\'#term_homepage\'>homepage</a></code> of http://danbri.org/; W3C is a \n<code><a href=\'#term_Organization\'>Organization</a></code> with a <code><a href=\'#term_homepage\'>homepage</a></code> of http://www.w3.org/. This \nallows us to say that Dan has a <code><a href=\'#term_workplaceHomepage\'>workplaceHomepage</a></code> of http://www.w3.org/.\n</p>\n\n<pre>\n&lt;foaf:Person&gt;\n &lt;foaf:name>Dan Brickley&lt;/foaf:name&gt;\n &lt;foaf:workplaceHomepage rdf:resource="http://www.w3.org/"/&gt;\n&lt;/foaf:Person&gt;\n</pre>\n</div>\n\n\n<p>\nNote that several other FOAF properties work this way; \n<code><a href=\'#term_schoolHomepage\'>schoolHomepage</a></code> is the most similar. In general, FOAF often indirectly \nidentifies things via Web page identifiers where possible, since these identifiers are widely \nused and known. FOAF does not currently have a term for the name of the relation (eg. \n"workplace") that holds \nbetween a <code><a href=\'#term_Person\'>Person</a></code> and an <code><a href=\'#term_Organization\'>Organization</a></code> that they work for.\n</p>\n\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_workplaceHomepage">#</a>] <!-- workplaceHomepage workplaceHomepage --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm " id="term_yahooChatID" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/yahooChatID" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:yahooChatID</h3> \n            <em>Yahoo chat ID</em> - A Yahoo chat ID <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >testing</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Agent"><a href="#term_Agent">Agent</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            \n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr><tr><th colspan="2">Inverse Functional Property</th>\n <td> <span rel="rdf:type" href="http://www.w3.org/2002/07/owl#InverseFunctionalProperty"></span> </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_yahooChatID\'>yahooChatID</a></code> property relates a <code><a href=\'#term_Agent\'>Agent</a></code> to a textual \nidentifier assigned to them in the Yahoo online Chat system. \nSee Yahoo\'s the <a href="http://chat.yahoo.com/">Yahoo!
