}">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >unstable</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Document"><a href="#term_Document">Document</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            \n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_sha1\'>sha1</a></code> property relates a <code><a href=\'#term_Document\'>Document</a></code> to the textual form of \na SHA1 hash of (some representation of) its contents.\n</p>\n\n<p class="editorial">\nThe design for this property is neither complete nor coherent. The <code><a href=\'#term_Document\'>Document</a></code> \nclass is currently used in a way that allows multiple instances at different URIs to have the \n\'same\' contents (and hence hash). If <code><a href=\'#term_sha1\'>sha1</a></code> is an owl:InverseFunctionalProperty, \nwe could deduce that several such documents were the self-same thing. A more careful design is \nneeded, which distinguishes documents in a broad sense from byte sequences.\n</p>\n\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_sha1">#</a>] <!-- sha1 sha1 --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm " id="term_status" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/status" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:status</h3> \n            <em>status</em> - A string expressing what the user is happy for the general public (normally) to know about their current activity. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >unstable</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Agent"><a href="#term_Agent">Agent</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            \n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\n<code><a href=\'#term_status\'>status</a></code> is a short textual string expressing what the user is happy\nfor the general public (normally) to know about their current activity. mood, location, etc.\n</p>\n\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_status">#</a>] <!-- status status --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div>\n<div class="specterm propertyterm archaic " id="term_dnaChecksum" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/dnaChecksum" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:dnaChecksum</h3> \n            <em>DNA checksum</em> - A checksum for the DNA of some thing. font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_dnaChecksum">#</a>] <!-- dnaChecksum dnaChecksum --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm archaic " id="term_family_name" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/family_name" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:family_name</h3> \n            <em>family_name</em> - The family name of some person. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >archaic</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Person"><a href="#term_Person">Person</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            \n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThis property is considered an <em>archaic</em> spelling of <code><a href=\'#term_familyName\'>familyName</a></code>.\n</p>\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_family_name">#</a>] <!-- family_name family_name --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm archaic " id="term_fundedBy" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/fundedBy" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:fundedBy</h3> \n            <em>funded by</em> - An organization funding a project or person. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >archaic</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://www.w3.org/2002/07/owl#Thing"><a href="#term_Thing">Thing</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Range:</th>\n <td> every value of this property is a  <span rel="rdfs:range" href="http://www.w3.org/2002/07/owl#Thing"><a href="#term_Thing">Thing</a></span>\n</td>\t</tr>\n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_fundedBy\'>fundedBy</a></code> property relates something to something else that has provided funding for it.\n</p>\n\n<p class="editorial">\nThis property is tentatively considered <em>archaic usage</em>, unless we hear about positive implementation experience.\n</p>\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_fundedBy">#</a>] <!-- fundedBy fundedBy --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm archaic " id="term_geekcode" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/geekcode" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:geekcode</h3> \n            <em>geekcode</em> - A textual geekcode for this person, see http://www.geekcode.com/geek.html <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >archaic</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Person"><a href="#term_Person">Person</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            \n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_geekcode\'>geekcode</a></code> property is used to represent a \'Geek Code\' for some \n<code><a href=\'#term_Person\'>Person</a></code>. \n</p>\n\n<p>\nSee the <a href="http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geek_Code">Wikipedia entry</a> for \ndetails of the code, which provides a somewhat frivolous and willfully obscure mechanism for \ncharacterising technical expertise, interests and habits. The <code><a href=\'#term_geekcode\'>geekcode</a></code> \nproperty is not bound to any particular version of the code. \n</p>\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_geekcode">#</a>] <!-- geekcode geekcode --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm archaic " id="term_givenname" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/givenname" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:givenname</h3> \n            <em>Given name</em> - The given name of some person. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >archaic</span></td></tr>\n            \n            \n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_givenName\'>givenName</a></code> property is provided (alongside <code><a href=\'#term_familyName\'>familyName</a></code>) for use when describing parts of people\'s names. Although these\nconcepts do not capture the full range of personal naming styles found world-wide, they are commonly used and have some value. \n</p>\n<p>\nThere is also a simple <code><a href=\'#term_name\'>name</a></code> property.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nSupport is also provided for the more archaic and culturally varying terminology of <code><a href=\'#term_firstName\'>firstName</a></code> and <code><a href=\'#term_lastName\'>lastName</a></code>.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nSee the <a href="http://wiki.foaf-project.org/w/NameVocabIssue">issue tracker</a> for design discussions, status and ongoing work on rationalising the FOAF\nnaming machinery.\n</p>\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_givenname">#</a>] <!-- givenname givenname --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm archaic " id="term_holdsAccount" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/holdsAccount" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:holdsAccount</h3> \n            <em>account</em> - Indicates an account held by this agent. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >archaic</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Agent"><a href="#term_Agent">Agent</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Range:</th>\n <td> every value of this property is a  <span rel="rdfs:range" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/OnlineAccount"><a href="#term_OnlineAccount">Online Account</a></span>\n</td>\t</tr>\n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p class="editorial">\nThis property is considered <em>archaic usage</em>. It is generally better to use <code><a href=\'#term_account\'>account</a></code> instead.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_holdsAccount\'>holdsAccount</a></code> property relates a <code><a href=\'#term_Agent\'>Agent</a></code> to an \n<code><a href=\'#term_OnlineAccount\'>OnlineAccount</a></code> for which they are the sole account holder. See \n<code><a href=\'#term_OnlineAccount\'>OnlineAccount</a></code> for usage details.\n</p>\n<p>\nThis property is equivalent to the <code><a href=\'#term_account\'>account</a></code> property, which \nwas introduced primarily to provide simpler naming for the same idea.\n</p>\n\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_holdsAccount">#</a>] <!-- holdsAccount holdsAccount --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm archaic " id="term_surname" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/surname" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:surname</h3> \n            <em>Surname</em> - The surname of some person. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >archaic</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/Person"><a href="#term_Person">Person</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            \n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            \n\n<p>A number of naming constructs are under development to provide\nnaming substructure; draft properties include <code><a href=\'#term_firstName\'>firstName</a></code>,\n<code><a href=\'#term_givenName\'>givenName</a></code>, and <code><a href=\'#term_surname\'>surname</a></code>. These are not currently \nstable or consistent; see the <a href="http://wiki.foaf-project.org/w/IssueTracker">issue \ntracker</a> for design discussions, status and ongoing work on rationalising the FOAF \nnaming machinery.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nThere is also a simple <code><a href=\'#term_name\'>name</a></code> property.\n</p>\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_surname">#</a>] <!-- surname surname --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div><div class="specterm propertyterm archaic " id="term_theme" about="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/theme" typeof="rdf:Property">\n            <h3>Property: foaf:theme</h3> \n            <em>theme</em> - A theme. <br /><table style="th { float: top; }">\n\t    <tr><th>Status:</th>\n\t    <td><span property="vs:status" >archaic</span></td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Domain:</th>\n <td>having this property implies being a  <span rel="rdfs:domain" href="http://www.w3.org/2002/07/owl#Thing"><a href="#term_Thing">Thing</a></span>\n</td></tr>\n            <tr><th>Range:</th>\n <td> every value of this property is a  <span rel="rdfs:range" href="http://www.w3.org/2002/07/owl#Thing"><a href="#term_Thing">Thing</a></span>\n</td>\t</tr>\n <tr><td>  <span rel="rdfs:isDefinedBy" href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/0.1/" />\n </td></tr>\n            </table>\n            <p class="editorial">\nThis property is considered <em>archaic usage</em>, and is not currently recommended for usage.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nThe <code><a href=\'#term_theme\'>theme</a></code> property is rarely used and under-specified. The intention was \nto use it to characterise interest / themes associated with projects and groups. Further \nwork is needed to meet these goals.\n</p>\n\n            <p style="float: right; font-size: small;">[<a href="#term_theme">#</a>] <!-- theme theme --> [<a href="#glance">back to top</a>]</p>\n            <br/>\n            </div>\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n</div>\n</div>\n\n\n<h2 id="sec-extrefs">External Vocabulary References</h2>\n\n<p>\nThe description of the terms in the FOAF \'dictionary\' often make reference to classes and properties elsewhere. This section of the FOAF specification provides a placeholder reference for any FOAF mention of externally defined terms. For example, sometimes we might say that FOAF property has a domain or range of an externally defined class, or that a FOAF class is a sub-class of an external class, or \'disjoint with\' such a class (ie. has no common members). Such claims help fix the intended meaning of FOAF terms in relationship to other \'peer\' vocabularies. \n</p>\n\n\n<h3 id="extns_status">Status Vocabulary</h3>\n\n<p>\nEach term in FOAF is annotated with properties from the <a href="http://www.w3.org/2003/06/sw-vocab-status/note">SemWeb Vocab Status Ontology</a>\n</p>\n\n<p>\nThis was created as an experiment in documenting FOAF\'s term-centric versioning model, in which a common fixed namespace URI is used, while term definitions slowly and independently evolve through different stability levels. This contrasts with other approaches to versioning which attach versioning information to larger sets of terms.\n</p>\n<p>\nNote that this mechanism is itself somewhat experimental and evolvin. The definitions of \'stable\', \'unstable\', \'archaic\' and \'testing\' cannot be defined as global absolutes, but only in relationship to the practices, expectations and social structures around some vocabulary. For their use in FOAF, future versions of this specification could usefully offer more detail about what to expect from a term labelled \'stable\'. \n </p>\n\n<dl>\n<dt>vs:term_status</dt>\n<dd>The vs:term_status property indicates the status of a vocabulary term, one of \'stable\',\'unstable\',\'testing\' or \'archaic\'. </dd>\n</dl>\n\n\n<div id="term_SpatialThing"/>\n<h3 id="extns_w3cbasicgeo">W3C Basic Geo (WGS84 lat/long) Vocabulary</h3>\n\n<p>Members of the FOAF and W3C Semantic Web Interest Group communities collaborated in 2003 to create a very simple vocabulary that described \npoints in geographic space. This is the <a href="http://www.w3.org/2003/01/geo/">W3CBasic Geo Vocabulary</a>. It assumes use of the WGS84 reference\nsystem and defines properties geo:lat, geo:long and geo:alt in terms of a class geo:SpatialThing.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nThe <a href="#term_based_near">foaf:based_near</a> property relates a spatial thing (typically a foaf:Agent of some kind) to another spatial thing,\nwhich can be described using geo:lat, geo:long etc.\n</p>\n\n<h3 id="extns_w3ccore">RDF Vocabulary Description - core concepts</h3>\n\n<p>\nThe FOAF dictionary of terms is defined using a family of W3C standards: RDF, RDF Schema and OWL. These share a data model and general approach, and \nprovide for increasing levels of expressivity. Here we introduce the core OWL and RDF/S terms used directly in the machine-readable description of FOAF. See \n<a href="http://www.w3.org/2001/sw/">W3C\'s site</a> for the latest and most authoritative OWL and RDF specifications.\n</p>\n\n<p>\nFOAF is based on the exchange of free-form descriptions that are structured in terms of things having properties, where the value of each property is \nexpressed as either textually (eg. a name or number), or by reference to another thing. FOAF (as an application of RDF) uses URI identifiers wherever \npossible to talk about things of interest, whether they are Web pages, classes of thing, properties of things, or even people. See the W3C Web Architecture specification for <a href="http://www.w3.org/TR/webarch/#identification">more background on URIs</a>.\n</p>\n<p>\nFrom core RDF, FOAF takes the notion that we are talking about things, and they fall into categories; we call these \'classes\'. The core machinery we \nuse from the RDF Schema and OWL technologies simply give us some built-in terminology for talking about things, classes and properties. Here we introduce\nsome of these and discuss briefly how they relate to FOAF\'s approach to describing things.\n</p>\n\n<dl>\n<dt id="term_Thing">owl:Thing</dt>\n<dd>OWL introduces the class \'Thing\' as a name for the universal class of all things. This is sometimes useful when\nwe want to express universality of property use, eg. that anything can be the value of <a href="#term_depicts">foaf:depicts</a>.</dd>\n<dt>rdf:Property</dt>\n<dd>RDF has a built-in class called rdf:Property. This is the class of all things like foaf:homepage or dc:creator which define named kinds of \nrelationship between pairs of things, or between things and textually-expressed information.</dd>\n<dt>owl:DatatypeProperty</dt>\n<dd>The OWL specifications give a name for those properties whose values are textually-expressed: "DataTypeProperty". RDF allows these to be either plain literal values (these can also carry an indicator of their language, via xml:lang), or else "data-typed", which means they are marked with a URI indicating \ntheir type (but no language tagging).</dd>\n<dt>owl:ObjectProperty</dt>\n<dd>ObjectProperty is OWL\'s name for those properties which are not textually-expressed; instead, they are used when mentioning or referring to \nsome other thing. OWL encourages vocabularies to avoid using a single named property in both \'ObjectProperty\' and \'DataTypeProperty\' styles. However\nearlier usage, notably in the Dublin Core community, does just this. Each FOAF property is either an Object Property or DataType Property.\n</dd>\n<dt>rdf:type</dt>\n<dd>One of the most commonly used built-in relationships in RDF is \'type\'. RDF type relates something to a class that it is in.</dd>\n<dt>rdfs:subClassOf</dt>\n<dd>RDFS gives a name for the relationship between some specific class and its more general superclass: \'subClassOf\'. It would have perhaps been \nsimpler if this was called \'superProperty\'. So for example we say that foaf:Person has an rdfs:subClassOf property whose value is foaf:Agent.\n</dd>\n\n\n<dt>rdfs:Class</dt>\n<dd>RDFS gives the name \'Class\' to those things that represent classes of thing, ie. which are values of rdf:type for their members. The \nOWL language also (for technical reasons) defines owl:Class for essentially the same notion. OWL also includes powerful machinery for defining the \nmembership rules for classes. This is not heavily used in FOAF, beyond the experimental mechanisms associated with foaf:Group.\n</dd>\n\n<dt>rdfs:subClassOf</dt>\n<dd>RDFS gives a name for the relationship between some particular class and its more general superclass: \'subClassOf\'. It would have perhaps been \nsimpler if this was called \'superProperty\'. So for example we say that foaf:Person has a subPropertyOf property whose value is foaf:Agent.\n</dd>\n\n\n<dt>rdfs:subPropertyOf</dt>\n<dd>Similar to subClassOf but for hierarchies of properties, we can use rdfs:subPropertyOf to point to a more general super-property, for example\nwe say foaf:aimChatID rdfs:subPropertyOf foaf:nick.\n</dd>\n\n<dt>rdfs:domain</dt>\n<dd>The RDFS specification introduced the notion of a property\'s domain. This is a way of saying, for some property, something about the \nkind of classes it is used with. If you know the domain or domains for some property, you know that whenever you see that property applied to something,\nthen that thing ought to be a member of those classes. Note that this does not mean that every description using the property is compelled to mention\nall those classes, just that the meaning of the property implies also the type of the thing the property is applied to. \n</dd>\n<dt>rdfs:range</dt>\n<dd>RDFS also defines a property of properties called \'range\'; this works just like rdfs:domain, except for the values of a property. If you know the\nrange of some property, you know what kinds of thing are reasonable values for it.\n</dd>\n<dt>owl:FunctionalProperty</dt>\n<dd>OWL provides even more useful information about properties, such as the ability to say that a property is \'functional\'. This means simply that\nfor any particular thing, you can expect at most one value for that property. It is simplest to think of this as \ncontextualised to any given time; although OWL doesn\'t talk about time explicitly. So we might say that \'age\' is functional, even though a series of \nFOAF documents might be published, each truthfully giving different values for my \'age\' which made sense in their original context. At the time of \nwriting, the only W3C technology that can take a larger perspective on such different perspectives / views (or \'graphs\') is SPARQL. If you have two \ndifferent values for a given functional property, you know you have a problem; perhaps one is out-of-date, for example. Or perhaps they only differ in\ntrivial detail (eg. date syntax, whitespace). \n</dd>\n<dt>owl:InverseFunctionalProperty</dt>\n<dd>\nOWL also gives a name for properties where common values tell us something about the identity of the thing having the property. On the Web this\ncan be very useful. OWL tells us that two descriptions are of the same thing, if they include truthful mention of some \'inverse functional property\'\nthat has the same value. The classic FOAF example could be two mentions of a person having some particular foaf:homepage. This OWL construct is very useful for reasoning about identity and merging scattered and partial descriptions.\n</dd>\n\n<dt>owl:inverseOf</dt>\n<dd>OWL provides a property \'inverseOf\' that holds between inverse properties; for example, any two things related by foaf:maker are related in \nthe reverse direction by foaf:made.</dd>\n\n<dt>owl:disjointWith</dt>\n<dd>OWL also lets us indicate that two classes have no common members. This can be useful for clarifying modelling assumptions in a language-neutral manner; eg. we might ask whether anything can be both a foaf:Group and a foaf:Organization simultaneously. \n</dd>\n\n<dt>owl:sameAs</dt>\n<dd>\nOWL provides a term for talking about identity in the sense of "being one and the same thing as". Many FOAF constructs imply \nthat two seemingly different things are in fact mentions of the self-same real world entity; this can be written out explicitly\nusing owl:sameAs. For example, if two FOAF descriptions give URI identifiers http://example.com/foo#person1 and http://example.org/bar#person2 in two person-descriptions, but ascribe the same homepage URI, we can conclude that person1 is owl:sameAs person2 (and vice-versa). This construct provides a powerful mechanism for decentralised, <a href="http://www.w3.org/DesignIssues/LinkedData">linked data</a>.\n</dd>\n\n<dt id="term_Concept">skos:Concept</dt>\n<dd>\n\tW3C provides a rich framework for describing linked topics, <a href="http://www.w3.org/2004/02/skos/">SKOS</a>. FOAF defines a\n\tsmall extension to SKOS called <a href="#term_focus">foaf:focus</a> designed to link conceptualisations of entities to more \n\tlinked data (eg. in FOAF) about the specific thing a SKOS "Concept" is about. \'Concept\' is SKOS\'s fundamental class, and \n\tcorresponds to abstractions common in library and cultural heritage information systems, including thesauri and subject indexing schemes. \n\t\n\t</dd>\n</dl>\n\n<h3 id="extns_dc">Dublin Core terms</h3>\n\n<p>\nThe Dublin Core specification provides term definitions that focus on issues of resource discovery, document description and related concepts useful for\ncultural heritage and digital library applications. FOAF can be used alongside any variants of Dublin Core, but works most effectively with the most \nmodern <a href="http://dublincore.org/documents/dcmi-terms/">Dublin Core terms</a> namespace. Note that here we use the prefix \'dct:\' to stand for the DC Terms namespace; however \nit is not unusual to see \'dc\' also used.\n</p>\n\n<dl>\n<dt>\n<a id="ext_dct_Agent" href="http://dublincore.org/documents/dcmi-terms/#classes-Agent">dct:Agent</a>\n</dt>\n<dd>\nDublin Core\'s notion of Agent is much like FOAF\'s; Dublin Core says "A resource that acts or has the power to act.", we say "things that do stuff". As nobody has \nprovided a counter-example of something fitting one definition but not the other, we say here that foaf:Agent stands in an \'equivalent class\' relationship to dct:Agent (and vice-versa).\n</dd>\n\n<dt>\n<a id="ex_dct_creator" href="http://dublincore.org/documents/dcmi-terms/#terms-creator">dct:creator</a>\n</dt>\n<dd>\nThe notion of \'creator\' in the latest versions of Dublin Core matches FOAF\'s notion of \'maker\'; based on their definitions, \nevery pair of things that are related by one of those properties are also related by the other. We express this by saying that \nthese properties stand in an \'equivalent property\' relationship\' to one another. \n</dd>\n</dl>\n\n\n\n<h3 id="extns_wn">Wordnet terms</h3>\n\n<p>\nEarlier versions of this specification used an experimental companion namespace produced from the lexical database Wordnet (v1.6). This is \ncurrently offline, and corresponding sub-class relationships have been ommited from the FOAF documentation. More <a href="http://www.w3.org/TR/wordnet-rdf/">recent</a> RDF representations of Wordnet now exist, however they don\'t map Wordnet synsets to classes, so can\'t be directly used here. Future\nversions of this specification might restore links to some version of Wordnet in RDF.\n</p>\n\n<h3 id="extns_sioc">SIOC terms</h3>\n\n<p>\nMany terms in the <a href="http://rdfs.org/sioc/ns#">SIOC</a> vocabulary are defined with reference to FOAF. See the <a href="http://www.sioc-project.org/">SIOC</a> project for details. Future versions of this specification may provide more information here.\n</p>\n\n  <h3 id="sec-ack">Acknowledgments</h3>\n\n  <p>There are far too many people who have contributed to the FOAF\n  project to name everyone in this early-release of the new\n  improved spec. FOAF wouldn\'t be such a fun project or be as\n  widely known as it is today without the efforts, enthusiasm and\n  intelligence of the folks who have contributed via the <a href=\n  "http://rdfweb.org/pipermail/rdfweb-dev/">rdfweb-dev</a> list,\n  <a href="http://rdfweb.org/irc/">#foaf</a> IRC channel, and wiki.</p>\n\n  <p>That said, a few milestones in FOAF\'s history should be\n  mentioned. We owe particular thanks to Edd Dumbill for his IBM\n  developerWorks articles (which attracted the affections of the\n  Weblogging crowd) and for his Foafbot application whose evolution\n  those articles have tracked. Also Morten Frederiksen\'s <a href=\n  "http://xml.mfd-consult.dk/foaf/explorer/">FoafExplorer</a>,\n  Daniel Krech\'s <a href="http://eikeon.com/foaf/">Web View</a>\n  aggregator, Jim Ley and Liz Turner\'s work on FOAFNaut, which alongside \n  FOAFbot have been instrumental in showing how FOAF data can be\n  collected and used. Meanwhile Leigh Dodd\'s <a href=\n  "http://www.ldodds.com/foaf/foaf-a-matic.html">foaf-a-matic</a>\n  has been the data creation tool that has been most people\'s\n  gateway to FOAFdom. FOAF also owes a lot to the folks at <a href=\n  "http://www.ecademy.com/">Ecademy</a>, <a href=\n  "http://www.typepad.com/">TypePad</a> and elsewhere for showing\n  how end users can share FOAF self-descriptions on the Web without\n  ever seeing a line of XML syntax. Jo Walsh has \n  enthused <a href=\n  "http://conferences.oreillynet.com/cs/et2003/view/e_sess/3633">many</a>\n  about hooking FOAF up to Geo and mapping data, as has Matt\n  Biddulph by <a href="http://hackdiary.com/">explaining</a> the\n  workings of his FOAF harvesting and image metadata tools. FOAF\n  has also benefited greatly from documentation contributed in\n  non-English languages, many thanks to all contributors of\n  translations (foaf-a-matic and other docs). FOAF is now arguably\n  better documented <a href=\n  "http://kanzaki.com/docs/sw/foaf.html">in Japanese</a> and\n  <a href=\n  "http://f14web.com.ar/inkel/2003/01/27/foaf.html">Spanish</a>\n  than in English, thanks to Masahide Kanzaki and Leandro Mariano\n  Lopez (inkel) respectively. Thanks also to <a href=\n  "http://crschmidt.net/">Chris Schmidt</a> for fixing up\n  the <a href="../0.1/specgen.py">spec generation</a> tool (now a\n  Python/Redland script), as well as for contributing numerous\n  <a href="http://crschmidt.net/semweb/">cool hacks</a> to the FOAF\n  community. To Richard Cyganiak and others in IRC for (amongst much else) help debugging \n  Apache configurations. To Ian Davis for his wonderful <a href="http://iandavis.com/2006/foaf-icons/">FOAF Logo</a>. And last but not least, Marc Canter is in a class of his own. Thanks all,\n  and to those who aren\'t listed here yet, but who made a difference...\n</p>\n\n  <p>This brief survey only scratches the surface of a growing body\n  of work. Sincere thanks to all who have contributed tools,\n  documentation, brain cells and enthusiasm to this project. We\n  should also mention that FOAF would not be possible without the\n  collaborative and opensource efforts of the RDF developer\n  community, both in terms of idea sharing (#swig etc) and freely\n  available tools (Jena, Redland, RDFlib, Cwm, Sesame, 3store\n  etc).</p>\n\n  <p>Thanks also to TimBL, who dreamed most of this up <a href="http://www.w3.org/History/1989/proposal.html">years ago</a>, for seeing what "hypertext flexibility" could bring...</p>\n\n<h2 id="sec-changes">Recent Changes</h2>\n\n\n\n<h3 id="sec-changes20140114">Changes in version 0.99 (2014-01-14)</h3>\n<ul>\n\t<li>\'Document\' type status changed from \'testing\' to \'stable\'</li>\n\t<li>\'Image\' type status changed from \'testing\' to \'stable\'</li>\n\t<li>\'weblog\' property status changed from \'testing\' to \'stable\'</li>\n\t<li>\'page\' property status changed from \'testing\' to \'stable\'</li>\n        <li>Expressed equivalence mappings from FOAF to <a href="http://schema.org/">schema.org</a>: \n<a href="http://schema.org/Person">Person</a>, Image (<a href="http://schema.org/ImageObject">ImageObject</a>),\nDocument (<a href="http://schema.org/CreativeWork">CreativeWork</a>).</li>\n\t<li>Removed many links to the FOAF wiki, due to spam problems.</li>\n</ul>\n\n<h3 id="sec-changes20100809">2010-08-09</h3>\n<ul>\n\t<li>"The FOAF RDF namespace" clarified as "The FOAF RDF namespace URI".</li>\n\t<li>clarified that RDF/XML now available by conneg and direct link, but not embedded; and that inline RDFa is partial.</li>\n\t<li>swapped order of a couple of paragraphs from SOTD section; technical detail comes later now.</li>\n\t<li>added sepia (#5E2612) to style.css, for use as \'archaic\' background color on old terms.</li>\n\t<li>added <a href="#term_focus">foaf:focus</a> "The <code>foaf:focus</code> property relates a conceptualisation of something to the thing itself." We stay quiet for now on whether it is functional, inverse functional, ...</li>\n\t<li>added mention of SKOS Concept to index.rdf and to the \'external vocabs\' section of the HTML spec.</li>\n\t<li>added brief description of owl:sameAs to the \'external vocabs\' section.</li>\n\t<li>created basic wiki Conneg page and linked from mention in spec of Content negotiation.</li>\n\t<li>Added explanatory text alongside \'domain\' and \'range\', inspired by, and borrowing from, the <a href="http://vocab.org/bio/0.1/.html">Bio vocabulary</a>.</li>\n\t<li>removed link to Jim Ley\'s old SVG image annotation demo, as it isn\'t maintained.</li>\n\t<li>moved large section on FOAF and RDF into the Wiki.</li>\t\n\t<li>fixed a mistaken reference to isPrimarySubjectOf (where \'...topic\' was intended).</li>\n\t<li><a href="#term_topic_interest">topic_interest</a>: removed placeholder health warning and added a simple definition</li>\n\t<li>index.rdf: From unstable to testing: <a href="#term_Project">Project</a> (we should review DOAP usage and data too), <a href="#term_OnlineAccount">OnlineAccount</a> (should we specify URI as profile page?), <a href="#term_based_near">based_near</a> (time to review AR/POI use cases?), <a href="#term_title">title</a> (vs. honorific prefix?), <a href="#term_skypeID">skypeID</a>, <a href="#term_lastName">lastName</a>, <a href="#term_openid">openid</a>, <a href="#term_account">account</a>, <a href="#term_accountServiceHomepage">accountServiceHomepage</a>, <a href="#term_accountName">accountName</a> (would simple \'name\' work?),\n\t</li>\n\t<li>index.rdf: From unstable to archaic: <a href="#term_dna_checksum">dna_checksum</a> (joke isn\'t funny any more...)\n\t</li>\n\t<li>index.rdf: From testing to archaic: <a href="#term_surname">surname</a> (tidying clutter from the early days)</li>\n    <li>index.rdf: From testing to stable: <a href="#term_primaryTopic">primaryTopic</a>/isPrimaryTopicOf, <a href="#term_knows">knows</a> (since these are widely used and definitions haven\'t changed or been contested)</li>\n\t<li>index.rdf: confirmed that <a href="#term_Image">Image</a> is a subClassOf <a href="#term_Document">Document</a></li>\n\t<li>index.rdf: removed some false disjointness claims: people can be documents (tatoos); agents can be documents (eg. java software agents)</li>\n\t<li>index.rdf: our local label for owl:Thing is now "Thing" not "a Thing" (largely a spec generation detail)</li>\n    <li>index.rdf: broadened <a href="#term_topic_interest">foaf:topic_interest</a> and <a href="#term_interest">foaf:interest</a> to have domain of Agent rather than Person, allowing for collective (group, project, organization) interests.</li>\n    <li>index.rdf: <a href="#term_logo">foaf:logo</a> is an Inverse Functional Property now</li>\n</ul>\n\n<h3>Changes from version <a href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/spec/20100101.html">0.97</a> and <a href="http://xmlns.com/foaf/spec/20091215.html">0.96</a></h3>\n\n\t<ul>\n\t<li><code><a href="#term_givenName">foaf:givenName</a></code> and <code><a href="#term_familyName">foaf:familyName</a></code> have been changed from <code><a href="#term_givenname">foaf:givenname</a></code> and <code><a href="#term_family_name">foaf:family_name</a></code> to make FOAF in line with usage of these terms in the <a href="http://portablecontacts.net/">Portable Contacts</a> format. The previous versions remain in the document, marked as \'archaic\'.</li>\n\t<li>Two little-used and poorly defined properties have been marked as \'archaic\': <code><a href="#term_fundedBy">foaf:fundedBy</a></code> and <code><a href="#term_theme">foaf:theme</a></code></li>\n\t<li><code><a href="#term_holdsAccount">foaf:holdsAccount</a></code> has been marked as \'archaic\' in favour of <code><a href="#term_account">foaf:account</a></code></li>\n\t</ul>\n\n\n<h3 id="sec-changes20091215">2009-12-15</h3>\n\n<ul>\n<li>terms: foaf:Agent owl:equivalentClass with Dublin Core terms namespace \'Agent\'; similarly foaf:maker and \'creator\' from DC. These use the most modern http://purl.org/dc/terms/ namespace</li>\n<li>editorial: added note about the use of \'archaic\'</li>\n<li>editorial: added a paragraph on changes in this version</li>\n<li>terms: family_name marked as archaic; replaced with familyName</li>\n<li>terms: marked fundedBy as archaic</li>\n<li>terms: marked givenname as archaic; replaced with givenName</li>\n<li>terms: marked holdAccount as archaic; replaced with account</li>\n<li>terms: changed myersBriggs from incorrect objectProperty to datatypeProperty</li>\n<li>terms: added openId into the rdf/xml version (it was present in the dated version but not in index.rdf)</li>\n<li>terms: marked theme as archaic</li>\n<li>editorial: fixed outdated references to RDF/XML</li>\n<li>editorial: updated acknowledgements</li>\n<li>terms: added text about terms used in foaf not in the foaf namespace</li>\n<li>editorial: fixed links and stray references to rdfweb-dev and #rdfig </li>\n<li>editorial: fixed rdfweb.org blog links (now pointing at archive.org where possible)</li>\n<li>editorial: fixed links to the wiki (stray /w/)</li>\n<li>editorial: fixed internal link to sec-glance</li>\n<li>editorial: fixed copyright date to 2009</li>\n\n</ul>\n\n<h3 id="sec-changes20071102">2007-11-02</h3>\n\n<ul>\n<li>terms: added <a href="#term_openid">foaf:openid</a></li>\n<li>editorial: updated links to the FOAF wiki</li>\n<li>editorial: spec now uses RDFa DTD declaration.</li>\n</ul>\n<h3 id="sec-changes20070524">2007-05-24</h3>\n<ul>\n<li>terms: Organization, Group, member, made, maker are now stable.</li>\n\n<li>editorial: Table of contents represents actual rather than desired structure. links fixed.</li>\n<li>process: Clarified that the specification version and URL are not 0.1; publishing at /foaf/spec</li>\n<li>editorial: Noted that the namespace URI will not change.
